<!-- assumption-1: body_md -->
\label{as:ergodic}
For every policy $\pi\in\Pi$, the Markov chain with kernel $P_\pi$ is irreducible and aperiodic, hence admits a unique stationary distribution $d^\pi\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal S)$ satisfying $d^\pi = d^\pi P_\pi$.
<!-- end assumption-1 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:uniform-d-bound}
Suppose Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic} holds. Then $\underline d := \inf_{\pi\in\Pi,\, s\in\mathcal S} d^\pi(s) > 0$, and under a uniform minorization condition, $\underline d$ admits an explicit lower bound (Appendix~\ref{ap:mixing-assumption-details}).
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- fact-1: body_md -->
\label{fact:avg-pdl}
Under Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic}, for any policies $\pi,\pi'$ we have
\[
    \rho(\pi') - \rho(\pi)
    \;=\;
    \mathbb{E}_{s\sim d^{\pi'}}
    \Bigl[
        \bigl\langle Q^\pi_s,\,\pi'_s - \pi_s\bigr\rangle
    \Bigr].
\]
<!-- end fact-1 -->
<!-- fact-2: body_md -->
\label{fact:avg-policy-gradient}
Viewing $\nabla_{\pi_s}\rho(\pi)$ as the gradient on the simplex, we may represent it as
\[
\nabla_{\pi_s}\rho(\pi)\;=\;d^\pi(s)Q^\pi_s.
\]
<!-- end fact-2 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
When $D$ is the KL divergence, which induces the local Fisher geometry on the simplex, \eqref{eq:pmd-update} reduces to a natural policy gradient step \citep{agarwal_theory_2020, xiao_convergence_2022}. When $D$ is the squared Euclidean distance, it reduces to projected $Q$-descent \citep{xiao_convergence_2022, sun_policy_2024}. We keep the general Bregman geometry in this paper.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- fact-3: body_md -->
\label{fact:three-point}
For any $p\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal A)$, the PMD update~\eqref{eq:pmd-update} satisfies
\[
\eta_t\langle \tilde Q^t_s,\,\pi_s^{t+1}-p\rangle
\le
D(p,\pi_s^t) - D(p,\pi_s^{t+1}) - D(\pi_s^{t+1},\pi_s^t).
\]
<!-- end fact-3 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:master}
Let $\{\pi^t\}$ be generated by~\eqref{eq:pmd-update} with $\pi^0_s \in \operatorname{rint}(\mathcal{P}(\mathcal A))$ for all $s \in \mathcal S$, and suppose $A_t \leq \bar A$, $B_t \leq \bar B$ for all $t \geq 0$ with constants $\bar A, \bar B \geq 0$.

\emph{(i) Non-asymptotic linear rate.} If $\sup_t \chi_t \leq \bar\chi$ for some $\bar\chi \in (1, \infty)$ and the step sizes satisfy $\eta_t \geq \tfrac{\bar\chi}{\bar\chi - 1}\, \eta_{t-1}$ for all $t \geq 0$ with some $\eta_{-1} > 0$, then for all $t \geq 0$
\begin{equation} \label{eq:master-nonasymp}
\bigl(\Delta_t - (\bar\chi\, \bar A + \bar B)\bigr)_+ \;\leq\; \Bigl(1 - \tfrac{1}{\bar\chi}\Bigr)^t \Bigl(\Delta_0 + \tfrac{D_0^\star}{\eta_{-1}\bar\chi}\Bigr),
\end{equation}
where $(x)_+ := \max\{x, 0\}$.

\emph{(ii) Asymptotic rate.} Let $\chi := \limsup_t \chi_t$ and suppose $D_0^\star < \infty$ and $\lim_{t\to\infty} \eta_t/\eta_{t-1} = \infty$ with some $\eta_{-1} > 0$. Then for every $\zeta > 0$,
\begin{equation} \label{eq:master-asymp}
\limsup_{t\to\infty}\, \bigl(\Delta_t - ((\chi+\zeta)\, \bar A + \bar B)\bigr)_+^{1/t} \;\leq\; 1 - \tfrac{1}{\chi+\zeta};
\end{equation}
hence, $\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Delta_t \leq \chi\, \bar A + \bar B$. If in addition $\bar A = 0$, then $\limsup_{t\to\infty}(\Delta_t - \bar B)_+^{1/t} \leq 1 - 1/\chi$.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
\label{rmk:initial-divergence}
Lemma~\ref{lem:master} is informative whenever $D_0^\star<\infty$, which part~(ii) assumes. Since the lemma assumes an interior $\pi^0$, $D_0^\star$ is finite whenever $F$ is finite on the closed simplex, even for a deterministic $\pi^\star$. This covers the two standard choices: the KL divergence and the squared Euclidean distance. The exception is a geometry whose $F$ diverges at the boundary, such as a log barrier. There, a deterministic $\pi^\star$ gives $D_0^\star=\infty$, and the bound of part~(i), though still valid, is vacuous.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- assumption-2: body_md -->
\label{as:uniform-critic-error}
There exists $\varepsilon_Q \ge 0$ such that $\sup_{t \geq 0}\|\widehat Q^t - Q^t\|_\infty \le \varepsilon_Q$.
<!-- end assumption-2 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:avg-pmd-linear-inexact-limsup}
Let $\{\pi^t\}_{t\ge 0}$ be generated by the PMD update \eqref{eq:pmd-update} with $\tilde Q^t = \widehat Q^t$, under Assumptions~\ref{as:ergodic} and~\ref{as:uniform-critic-error}. Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:master}(i),
\begin{equation} \label{eq:inexact-nonasymptotic-linear}
(\Delta_t - 2(\bar\chi + 1)\varepsilon_Q)_+ \;\le\; \Bigl(1 - \tfrac{1}{\bar\chi}\Bigr)^t \Bigl(\Delta_0 + \tfrac{D_0^\star}{\eta_{-1}\bar\chi}\Bigr) \qquad \forall\, t \ge 0;
\end{equation}
and under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:master}(ii), for every $\zeta > 0$,
\begin{equation} \label{eq:inexact-asymptotic-linear}
\limsup_{t\to\infty}\bigl(\Delta_t - 2(\chi+\zeta+1)\varepsilon_Q\bigr)_+^{1/t} \;\le\; 1 - \tfrac{1}{\chi+\zeta}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:exact-pmd}
For exact PMD ($\widehat Q^t = Q^t$), setting $\varepsilon_Q = 0$ in~\eqref{eq:inexact-asymptotic-linear} removes the $\zeta$-dependence from the floor, so taking $\zeta \downarrow 0$ gives the sharper asymptotic rate
\[
\limsup_{t\to\infty} \Delta_t^{1/t} \;\leq\; 1 - \tfrac{1}{\chi}.
\]
Moreover, $\Delta_t \to 0$, so by compactness of $\Pi$ and continuity of $\pi \mapsto \rho(\pi)$, every limit point of $\{\pi^t\}$ is optimal. Consequently, if $\{\pi^t\}_{t\ge 0}$ converges to some $\pi^\star$ (e.g., when the optimal policy is unique), then $\chi_t \to \chi = 1$ and exact PMD is \emph{superlinear}.
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:uniform-mix}
Under Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic}, there exists an integer $t_{\mathrm{mix}} \geq 1$ such that $\sup_{\mu_0 \in \mathcal{P}(\mathcal S)} \|\mu_0\, P_\pi^{t_{\mathrm{mix}}} - d^\pi\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \leq 1/4$ for every $\pi \in \Pi$, and consequently
\begin{equation} \label{eq:tmix-decay}
\sup_{\mu_0 \in \mathcal{P}(\mathcal S)} \bigl\|\mu_0\, P_\pi^k - d^\pi\bigr\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \;\leq\; 2^{-\lfloor k/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor} \qquad \forall\, k \geq 0,\; \forall\, \pi \in \Pi.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
\label{rmk:uniform-geometric-mixing}
Uniform mixing is usually posed as an assumption in average-reward reinforcement learning \citep[e.g.,][]{li_stochastic_2024, wang_optimal_2024}. In our finite setting, it follows from Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic} by compactness of $\Pi$. Appendix~\ref{ap:mixing-assumption-details} gives the proof and a sufficient condition under which $t_{\mathrm{mix}}$ is explicit.
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:Q_estimation_burnin}
Suppose Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic} holds. For any policy $\pi$, state--action pair $(s,a)$, target accuracy $\varepsilon_Q \in (0, 1)$, and confidence $\delta \in (0, 1)$, estimating $\hat\rho(\pi)$ and $\widehat Q^\pi(s,a)$ via~\eqref{eq:rho-estimation} and~\eqref{eq:q-estimation} with burn-in $\tau = \lceil 2H/3 \rceil$, $H = \widetilde\Theta(t_{\mathrm{mix}})$, and $B = \widetilde\Theta(t_{\mathrm{mix}}^2/\varepsilon_Q^2)$ guarantees $|\widehat Q^\pi(s,a) - Q^\pi(s,a)| \leq \varepsilon_Q$ with probability at least $1 - \delta$, using a total of
\[
N \;=\; BH \;=\; \widetilde O\bigl(t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3 / \varepsilon_Q^2\bigr)
\]
generative-model transitions.
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:tabular_lower_bound}
For every $p \in (0, 1/4)$, every $\varepsilon_Q \in (0, 1/(48 p)]$, and every (possibly adaptive) generative-model algorithm, there is a two-state, one-action average-reward MDP satisfying Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic} with $t_{\mathrm{mix}} = \Theta(1/p)$ on which outputting $\widehat Q \in \mathbb{R}^2$ with $\Pr(\|\widehat Q - Q\|_\infty \leq \varepsilon_Q) \geq 2/3$ requires $N \geq \Omega(t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3/\varepsilon_Q^2)$ generative-model queries. The explicit construction and constants are given in Appendix~\ref{ap:proofs-tabular}.
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:pmd_sample_complexity}
Suppose Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic} holds. For any target suboptimality $\varepsilon \in (0, 1)$ and confidence $\delta \in (0, 1)$, using the critic of Lemma~\ref{lem:Q_estimation_burnin} at accuracy $\varepsilon_Q = \varepsilon/(4(\bar\chi + 1))$ together with the actor of Theorem~\ref{thm:avg-pmd-linear-inexact-limsup} in \eqref{eq:inexact-nonasymptotic-linear} guarantees $\Delta_T \leq \varepsilon$ with probability at least $1 - \delta$ after $T = \widetilde\Theta(\bar\chi)$ iterations, using a total of
\[
N \;=\; \widetilde O\bigl(\bar\chi^3\, |\mathcal S|\,|\mathcal A|\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3 / \varepsilon^2\bigr)
\]
generative-model transitions.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
\label{rmk:tabular-sample-complexity}
By Prop.~\ref{prop:tabular_lower_bound}, the critic's $t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3/\varepsilon^2$ sample complexity in Thm.~\ref{thm:pmd_sample_complexity} is information-theoretically tight for any policy-gradient method whose critic satisfies Asm.~\ref{as:uniform-critic-error}. The $t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3$ exponent matches \citet[Cor.~1]{li_stochastic_2024} while strengthening their in-expectation, random-iterate guarantee to a high-probability, last-iterate one. In comparison, the $\widetilde O(|\mathcal S||\mathcal A|\,t_{\mathrm{mix}}/\varepsilon^2)$ rate of \citet{wang_optimal_2024} is achieved by model-based planning, which does not need a critic satisfying Asm.~\ref{as:uniform-critic-error} and is not directly applicable to the policy-gradient methods we analyze. \S\ref{sec:sample-complexity-non-tabular} further removes the $|\mathcal S||\mathcal A|$ factor with linear function approximation and needs only a single trajectory instead of a generative model.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
Although~\eqref{eq:pmd-update} is written over per-state distributions, the actor parameterization is implicit: for example, specializing $D$ to KL divergence yields the multiplicative update $\pi^{t+1}(a\mid s) \propto \pi^t(a\mid s)\exp(-\eta_t \phi_{s,a}^\top w_t)$, so from a uniform $\pi^0$ every iterate is a log-linear policy in the critic's features, recovering the policy class of \citet{yuan_linear_2023}. We retain the general Bregman presentation.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- assumption-3: body_md -->
\label{as:approx-error}
There exists $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}}\ge 0$ such that $\mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim\nu^t}[(w_t^{\star\top}\phi_{s,a}-Q^t(s,a))^2] \le \varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}}^2$ for all $t\ge 0$.
<!-- end assumption-3 -->
<!-- assumption-4: body_md -->
\label{as:stat-error}
There exists $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}\ge 0$ such that $\sup_{t\geq0}\mathbb{E}[\|w_t - w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}^2] \le \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2$.
<!-- end assumption-4 -->
<!-- assumption-5: body_md -->
\label{as:L2-mismatch-bound}
There exist finite deterministic constants $C_{+},C_{\star}<\infty$ such that, almost surely for all $t\ge 0$,
\[
\mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim \nu^t}\!\left[\left(\tfrac{\nu^{t+1}(s,a)}{\nu^t(s,a)}\right)^2\right]
\le C_{+},
\qquad
\mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim \nu^t}\!\left[\left(\tfrac{\nu^{\star}(s,a)}{\nu^t(s,a)}\right)^2\right]
\le C_{\star}.
\]
<!-- end assumption-5 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
\label{rmk:eps-exploration}
A simple sufficient condition to satisfy Assumption~\ref{as:L2-mismatch-bound} is to enforce an $\varepsilon$-exploration on every iterate so that $\pi^t(a\mid s)\ge \varepsilon/|\mathcal{A}|$ for all $(s,a)$. Then $\nu^t(s,a)\ge (\underline{d}\varepsilon)/|\mathcal{A}|$ and the assumption holds with $C_{+},C_{\star}= (|\mathcal{A}|/(\underline{d}\varepsilon))^2$, which can be loose and incurs an $O(\varepsilon)$ optimization bias.
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:non-tabular-linear-convergence}
Let $\{\pi^t\}_{t\ge 0}$ be generated by~\eqref{eq:pmd-update} with $\tilde Q^t(s,a) = \phi_{s,a}^\top w_t$ under Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic} and Assumptions~\ref{as:approx-error}--\ref{as:L2-mismatch-bound}. Set $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{floor}} := 2(\bar\chi\sqrt{C_+} + \sqrt{C_\star})(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}} + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}})$. Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:master}(i),
\[
(\mathbb{E}[\Delta_t] - \varepsilon_{\mathrm{floor}})_+ \;\le\; \Bigl(1 - \tfrac{1}{\bar\chi}\Bigr)^t\,\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[\Delta_0] + \tfrac{\mathbb{E}[D_0^\star]}{\eta_{-1}\bar\chi}\Bigr) \qquad \forall\, t \ge 0.
\]
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
Unlike Section~\ref{sec:tabular}, we do not characterize an asymptotic rate via $\chi=\limsup_{t\to\infty}\chi_t$. Here $w_t$ (and hence $\pi^t$) is random, which makes $\chi$ random, so such a statement would require additional uniform assumptions on the sample-path behavior of $\chi_t$, which we do not impose.
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:w-sample-complexity}
Suppose $\|\phi_{s,a}\|_2\le L$ for all $(s,a)$ and for all $t$, $\Sigma_t := \mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim \nu^{t}}\bigl[\phi_{s,a}\phi_{s,a}^\top\bigr] \succeq \mu I_d$
for some $\mu>0$. In addition, suppose Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic} holds. Fix an outer iteration $t$ and let $w_t$ be the output of Algorithm~\ref{alg:end2end_w_oracle} with step size
$\alpha=\frac{1}{4L^2}$. Then for any target critic accuracy $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}\in(0,1)$, the number of transitions
\[
N
\;=\;
\widetilde{O}\!\left(
\frac{t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3\,d\,(1+L^2/\mu)^2}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2}
\right)
\]
suffices to guarantee $\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}^2\right] \;\le\; \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2$.
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{prop:nontabular_lower_bound}
For every $d \geq 1$, $p \in (0, 1/4)$, $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}} \in (0, 1/(256\,p)]$, and every (possibly adaptive) generative-model algorithm, there is a one-action average-reward MDP on $2d$ states satisfying Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic} with $t_{\mathrm{mix}} = \Theta(1/p)$ and exactly realizable linear differential $Q$ on which outputting $\widehat w \in \mathbb{R}^d$ with $\Pr(\|\widehat w - w^\star\|_\Sigma^2 \leq \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2) \geq 2/3$ requires $N \geq \Omega(d\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2)$ generative-model queries. The explicit construction and constants are given in Appendix~\ref{ap:proofs-non-tabular}.
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
\label{rmk:covariance-condition}
The covariance condition $\Sigma_t \succeq \mu I_d$ of Lem.~\ref{lem:w-sample-complexity} can be guaranteed by the features alone. It holds with a single $\mu > 0$ for all policies if $\operatorname{span}\{\phi_{s,a_s} : s \in \mathcal S\} = \mathbb{R}^d$ for every choice of one action $a_s$ per state. When $d \le |\mathcal S|$, this is true for almost every choice of features. If the features lack this property, the $\varepsilon$-exploration of Remark~\ref{rmk:eps-exploration} enforces the condition whenever $\operatorname{span}\{\phi_{s,a} : (s,a) \in \mathcal S \times \mathcal A\} = \mathbb{R}^d$. Importantly, the condition does not make the problem easier: the hard instances of Prop.~\ref{prop:nontabular_lower_bound} satisfy it, so the matching lower bound holds under it. Appendix~\ref{ap:covariance-condition} gives the details.
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:non_tabular_pmd_sample_complexity_generative}
Assume the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:non-tabular-linear-convergence} and Lemma~\ref{lem:w-sample-complexity}. For any target accuracy $\varepsilon \in (0,1)$ satisfying $\varepsilon \ge 8(\bar\chi\sqrt{C_+}+\sqrt{C_\star})\varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}}$, using the critic of Lemma~\ref{lem:w-sample-complexity} at accuracy $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}} = \varepsilon/(8(\bar\chi\sqrt{C_+}+\sqrt{C_\star}))$ together with the actor of Theorem~\ref{thm:non-tabular-linear-convergence} guarantees $\mathbb{E}[\Delta_T] \le \varepsilon$ after $T = \widetilde\Theta(\bar\chi)$ outer iterations, using a total of
\[
N
\;=\;
\widetilde O\!\left(
\frac{t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3\,d\,L^4\,(C_{+}+C_{\star})\,\bar\chi^3}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\mu^2}
\right)
\]
transitions of a single trajectory.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
Beyond the $|\mathcal S||\mathcal A|$-free sample complexity, Theorem~\ref{thm:non_tabular_pmd_sample_complexity_generative} also operates under a strictly weaker oracle than Theorem~\ref{thm:pmd_sample_complexity}: only a single trajectory is needed, not a full generative model that queries arbitrary $(s,a)$ pairs. With the same single-trajectory oracle as \citet[Corollary~3]{li_stochastic_2024}, it improves their $t_{\mathrm{mix}}^5$ dependence to $t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3$ through the trade-off of Obstacle~1: a linear-rate actor with few accurate critic calls in place of a sublinear actor with many bias-controlled ones. By Proposition~\ref{prop:nontabular_lower_bound}, the $t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3/\varepsilon^2$ dependence of the critic's sample complexity in Theorem~\ref{thm:non_tabular_pmd_sample_complexity_generative} is information-theoretically tight for any policy-gradient method whose critic satisfies Assumption~\ref{as:stat-error}, even one with a generative model. The remaining $\bar\chi^3, (C_++C_\star)$ factors are standard PMD-path and distribution-mismatch quantities outside the critic's sample complexity.
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- fact-4: body_md -->
\label{fact:ap-poisson}
Under Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic}, for each policy $\pi$ there exist a scalar $\rho(\pi)$
and a function $h^\pi:\mathcal S\to\mathbb R$ such that
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ap-poisson}
\rho(\pi) + h^\pi \;=\; c_\pi + P_\pi h^\pi.
\end{equation}
The pair $(\rho(\pi),h^\pi)$ is unique up to adding a constant to $h^\pi$. Throughout the paper, we fix the \emph{centered} convention $d^\pi h^\pi = 0$, under which $h^\pi(s) = \lim_{H\to\infty} \mathbb{E}[\sum_{k=0}^{H-1}(c(s_k, a_k) - \rho(\pi)) \mid s_0 = s]$ and $Q^\pi(s,a) = c(s,a) - \rho(\pi) + \mathbb{E}[h^\pi(s_1) \mid s_0 = s, a_0 = a]$.
<!-- end fact-4 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
Fix a policy $\pi$, let $\mathbf 1 \in \mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|}$ denote the all-ones vector, and write $g \coloneqq c_\pi - \rho(\pi)\mathbf 1$. For an ergodic chain,~\eqref{eq:ap-poisson} has a solution, unique up to adding a constant to $h^\pi$; see, e.g., \citet{puterman_markov_1994} and \citet[Section~2]{li_stochastic_2024}. Applying $d^\pi$ to~\eqref{eq:ap-poisson} and using $d^\pi P_\pi = d^\pi$ gives $\rho(\pi) = d^\pi c_\pi$, so $d^\pi g = 0$, and the centered convention $d^\pi h^\pi = 0$ selects a unique $h^\pi$.

Finally, we verify the limit representation. Under Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic}, $P_\pi^k \to \mathbf 1 d^\pi$ geometrically fast (Lemma~\ref{lem:per-policy-mixing}), and $d^\pi g = 0$, so $P_\pi^k g = (P_\pi^k - \mathbf 1 d^\pi)\,g \to 0$ geometrically and $\tilde h \coloneqq \sum_{k\ge 0} P_\pi^k g$ converges. Telescoping gives $(I-P_\pi)\tilde h = g$, and $d^\pi\tilde h = \sum_{k\ge 0} d^\pi g = 0$, so $\tilde h = h^\pi$ by uniqueness under the centered convention. Since $(P_\pi^k g)(s) = \mathbb{E}[c(s_k,a_k)-\rho(\pi)\mid s_0=s]$, this is the stated limit for $h^\pi$; the expression for $Q^\pi$ follows by conditioning on the first transition in~\eqref{eq:diff-Q-rolling}.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
By the definition of $Q^\pi$ and~\eqref{eq:ap-poisson}, $\langle Q^\pi_s, \pi_s\rangle = h^\pi(s)$ and $\langle Q^\pi_s, \pi'_s\rangle = \langle c_s, \pi'_s\rangle - \rho(\pi) + \mathbb{E}[h^\pi(s_1) \mid s_0 = s, a_0 \sim \pi'_s]$. Subtracting, averaging over $s \sim d^{\pi'}$, and using the stationarity of $d^{\pi'}$ under $P_{\pi'}$ cancels the $h^\pi$ terms; $\rho(\pi') = \mathbb{E}_{s\sim d^{\pi'}}[\langle c_s, \pi'_s\rangle]$ then gives~\eqref{eq:ap-avg-pdl-qform}.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:ap-uniform-d-bound}
Suppose Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic} holds. Then we have
\[
\underline d \;:=\; \inf_{\pi\in\Pi, s\in\mathcal S} d^\pi(s) > 0.
\]
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Fix $\pi$. By irreducibility, $d^\pi(s) > 0$ for every $s$: if $d^\pi(s_0) = 0$, stationarity, $0 = \sum_s d^\pi(s) P_\pi(s_0 \mid s)$, forces $d^\pi(s) = 0$ wherever $P_\pi(s_0 \mid s) > 0$, and iterating along the chain gives $d^\pi \equiv 0$, a contradiction. Next, $\pi \mapsto d^\pi$ is continuous: $\pi \mapsto P_\pi$ is continuous, and $d^\pi$ is the unique solution of the linear system obtained from $d(P_\pi - I) = 0$ by replacing its last equation with $d\,\mathbf 1 = 1$; since $1$ is a simple eigenvalue of the ergodic matrix $P_\pi$, this system is nonsingular, and matrix inversion is continuous. Finally, $\Pi$ is compact, so the continuous function $(\pi, s) \mapsto d^\pi(s)$ attains its minimum on $\Pi \times \mathcal S$, which is positive; this minimum is $\underline d$.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
The proof has two parts. We first derive a one-step Lyapunov recursion that holds whenever the step-size and mismatch conditions are satisfied at time $t$ with some parameter $\bar\chi > 1$; then we iterate this recursion from $t = 0$ to obtain~(i), and iterate it on a tail with $\bar\chi = \chi + \zeta$ (for any fixed $\zeta > 0$) to obtain~(ii).

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{One-step recursion.}
Fix $t \geq 0$ and suppose $\chi_{t+1} \leq \bar\chi$ and $\eta_t/\eta_{t-1} \geq \bar\chi/(\bar\chi - 1)$ for some $\bar\chi > 1$. Applying Fact~\ref{fact:three-point} with $p = \pi^\star_s$ to the generalized PMD update~\eqref{eq:pmd-update} and dropping the nonnegative term $D(\pi^{t+1}_s, \pi^t_s) \geq 0$ gives
$\eta_t \langle \tilde Q^t_s, \pi^{t+1}_s - \pi^\star_s\rangle \leq D(\pi^\star_s, \pi^t_s) - D(\pi^\star_s, \pi^{t+1}_s)$.
Decompose $\pi^{t+1}_s - \pi^\star_s = (\pi^{t+1}_s - \pi^t_s) + (\pi^t_s - \pi^\star_s)$ and take expectation over $s \sim d^\star$:
\begin{equation} \label{eq:ap-master-step1}
\eta_t\,\mathbb{E}_{s\sim d^\star}\!\bigl[\langle \tilde Q^t_s, \pi^t_s - \pi^\star_s\rangle\bigr] + \eta_t\,\mathbb{E}_{s\sim d^\star}\!\bigl[\langle \tilde Q^t_s, \pi^{t+1}_s - \pi^t_s\rangle\bigr] \leq D_t^\star - D_{t+1}^\star.
\end{equation}
By Fact~\ref{fact:avg-pdl} and the definition of $B_t$,
$\mathbb{E}_{s\sim d^\star}[\langle \tilde Q^t_s, \pi^t_s - \pi^\star_s\rangle] \geq \Delta_t - B_t$.
By~\eqref{eq:pmd-sign} the integrand $\langle \tilde Q^t_s, \pi^{t+1}_s - \pi^t_s\rangle$ is pointwise nonpositive, so the change-of-measure ratio $d^\star(s)/d^{t+1}(s) \leq \bar\chi$ gives $\mathbb{E}_{s\sim d^\star}[\langle \tilde Q^t_s, \pi^{t+1}_s - \pi^t_s\rangle] \geq \bar\chi\,\mathbb{E}_{s\sim d^{t+1}}[\langle \tilde Q^t_s, \pi^{t+1}_s - \pi^t_s\rangle] \geq \bar\chi\bigl((\Delta_{t+1} - \Delta_t) - A_t\bigr)$, where the second inequality uses Fact~\ref{fact:avg-pdl} and the definition of $A_t$. Substituting both into~\eqref{eq:ap-master-step1} and rearranging,
\[
\Delta_{t+1} + \frac{D_{t+1}^\star}{\eta_t \bar\chi} \leq (1 - 1/\bar\chi)\Delta_t + \frac{D_t^\star}{\eta_t \bar\chi} + \frac{\bar\chi A_t + B_t}{\bar\chi}.
\]
The step-size growth condition gives $1/(\eta_t \bar\chi) \leq (1 - 1/\bar\chi) \cdot 1/(\eta_{t-1}\bar\chi)$, so defining the Lyapunov sequence $\Psi_t := \Delta_t + D_t^\star/(\eta_{t-1}\bar\chi)$ yields the one-step recursion
\begin{equation} \label{eq:ap-master-onestep}
\Psi_{t+1} \leq (1 - 1/\bar\chi)\,\Psi_t + \frac{\bar\chi A_t + B_t}{\bar\chi}.
\end{equation}

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@

  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Part (i): non-asymptotic rate.}
Under the hypotheses of~(i), the one-step recursion~\eqref{eq:ap-master-onestep} holds at every $t \geq 0$ with $\bar\chi$ the global uniform bound. Using $A_t \leq \bar A$, $B_t \leq \bar B$ and iterating from $t = 0$,
\[
\Psi_t \leq (1 - 1/\bar\chi)^t \Psi_0 + \frac{\bar\chi \bar A + \bar B}{\bar\chi}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}(1 - 1/\bar\chi)^i = (1 - 1/\bar\chi)^t \Psi_0 + (\bar\chi \bar A + \bar B)\bigl(1 - (1 - 1/\bar\chi)^t\bigr).
\]
Since $\Delta_t \leq \Psi_t$, subtracting $\bar\chi \bar A + \bar B$ and taking the positive part yields~\eqref{eq:master-nonasymp}.

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Part (ii): asymptotic rate.}
Let $\chi := \limsup_t \chi_t$ and fix $\zeta > 0$, setting $\hat\chi := \chi + \zeta$. By the $\limsup$ definition, $\chi_{t+1} \leq \hat\chi$ for all $t$ beyond some $t_0(\zeta)$. By $\eta_t/\eta_{t-1} \to \infty$, there exists $t_1(\zeta)$ such that $\eta_t/\eta_{t-1} \geq \hat\chi/(\hat\chi - 1)$ for $t \geq t_1(\zeta)$. Set $t_\zeta := \max\{t_0(\zeta), t_1(\zeta)\}$; both hypotheses of the one-step recursion hold with $\bar\chi = \hat\chi$ for all $t \geq t_\zeta$. Iterating~\eqref{eq:ap-master-onestep} from $t_\zeta$ gives
\[
\Psi_t \leq (1 - 1/\hat\chi)^{t - t_\zeta}\, \Psi_{t_\zeta} + (\hat\chi \bar A + \bar B)\bigl(1 - (1 - 1/\hat\chi)^{t - t_\zeta}\bigr) \qquad \forall\, t \geq t_\zeta.
\]
Hence $(\Delta_t - (\hat\chi \bar A + \bar B))_+ \leq (1 - 1/\hat\chi)^{t - t_\zeta}\, \Psi_{t_\zeta}$. Here $\Psi_{t_\zeta} < \infty$: $D_0^\star < \infty$, and the one-step inequality, which holds before the growth condition is invoked, keeps every $D_t^\star$ finite. Taking $t$-th roots, $\limsup$, and noting $\Psi_{t_\zeta}^{1/t} \to 1$ yields~\eqref{eq:master-asymp}: $\limsup_{t\to\infty}(\Delta_t - ((\chi+\zeta) \bar A + \bar B))_+^{1/t} \leq 1 - 1/(\chi+\zeta)$. The same bound also gives $\limsup_t \Delta_t \leq \hat\chi \bar A + \bar B$; letting $\zeta \downarrow 0$ yields $\limsup_t \Delta_t \leq \chi \bar A + \bar B$. When $\bar A = 0$ the floor $\hat\chi \bar A + \bar B$ reduces to $\bar B$ independently of $\zeta$, so~\eqref{eq:master-asymp} becomes $\limsup_{t\to\infty}(\Delta_t - \bar B)_+^{1/t} \leq 1 - 1/(\chi+\zeta)$; letting $\zeta \downarrow 0$ gives the sharper rate $1 - 1/\chi$.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:ap-avg-pmd-linear-inexact-limsup}
Suppose Assumptions~\ref{as:ergodic} and \ref{as:uniform-critic-error} hold, and let $\{\pi^t\}_{t\ge 0}$ be generated by the inexact PMD update \eqref{eq:pmd-update} with $\tilde Q^t = \widehat Q^t$. If there exists $\bar\chi\in(1,\infty)$ with $\sup_t \chi_t \leq \bar\chi$ and the step sizes satisfy $\eta_t \;\ge\; \frac{\bar\chi}{\bar\chi-1}\,\eta_{t-1}$ for all $t\ge 0$ with some $\eta_{-1}>0$, then for all $t\ge 0$,
\[
(\Delta_t-2(\bar\chi+1)\varepsilon_Q)_+
\;\le\;
\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{\bar\chi}\Bigr)^t
\Bigl(\Delta_0+\frac{1}{\eta_{-1}\bar\chi}D_0^\star\Bigr).
\]

Let $\chi:=\limsup_{t\to\infty}\chi_t$ and suppose $D_0^\star<\infty$ and the step sizes satisfy $\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\eta_t}{\eta_{t-1}}=\infty$ with some $\eta_{-1}>0$. Then for every $\zeta > 0$,
\[
\limsup_{t\to\infty}(\Delta_t - 2(\chi+\zeta+1)\varepsilon_Q)_+^{1/t} \;\le\; 1 - \frac{1}{\chi+\zeta}.
\]
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Here, $\tilde Q^t = \widehat Q^t$. Under Assumption~\ref{as:uniform-critic-error}, $\|\widehat Q^t - Q^t\|_\infty \leq \varepsilon_Q$; combined with $\|\pi_s - \pi'_s\|_1 \leq 2$ for any two policies, $|\langle \widehat Q^t_s - Q^t_s, \pi_s - \pi'_s\rangle| \leq 2\varepsilon_Q$. Taking expectations preserves this bound, so $A_t, B_t \leq 2\varepsilon_Q$ for all $t$.

Applying Lemma~\ref{lem:master}(i) with $\bar A = \bar B = 2\varepsilon_Q$ yields directly the non-asymptotic bound with floor $\bar\chi \bar A + \bar B = 2(\bar\chi + 1)\varepsilon_Q$; part~(ii) yields, for every $\zeta > 0$, $\limsup_t (\Delta_t - ((\chi+\zeta)\bar A + \bar B))_+^{1/t} \le 1 - 1/(\chi+\zeta)$ with floor $(\chi+\zeta)\bar A + \bar B = 2(\chi+\zeta+1)\varepsilon_Q$.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:ap-Q_estimation_burnin}
Suppose Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic} holds. Fix a policy $\pi$, a state--action pair $(s, a) \in \mathcal S \times \mathcal A$, a target accuracy $\varepsilon_Q \in (0, 1)$, and a confidence $\delta \in (0, 1)$. For $H \geq 4$, set $\tau := \lceil 2H/3 \rceil$ and $H_\tau := H - \tau$. Generate $B$ independent length-$H$ trajectories $\{x_k^{(i)}\}_{k=0}^{H-1}$ by setting $x_0^{(i)} = (s, a)$ and following $\pi$, and define
\[
\hat\rho_{\tau, H} \;:=\; \frac{1}{B\,H_\tau}\sum_{i=1}^B \sum_{k=\tau}^{H-1} c(x_k^{(i)}),
\qquad
\widehat Q_{H, \tau}(s, a) \;:=\; \frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^B \sum_{k=0}^{H-1}\bigl(c(x_k^{(i)}) - \hat\rho_{\tau, H}\bigr).
\]
There exist universal constants $C_1, C_2, C_3 > 0$ such that whenever
\[
H \;\geq\; C_2\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}\, \log(C_3\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}/\varepsilon_Q)
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
B \;\geq\; C_1\,\frac{H\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}}{\varepsilon_Q^2}\,\log\frac{2}{\delta},
\]
we have $|\widehat Q_{H, \tau}(s, a) - Q^\pi(s, a)| \leq \varepsilon_Q$ with probability at least $1 - \delta$.
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:ap-covariance-summability}
Fix a policy $\pi$ and a function $g : \mathcal S \times \mathcal A \to \mathbb{R}$ with $\mathbb{E}_{\nu^\pi}[g] = 0$, where $\nu^\pi(s, a) := d^\pi(s)\, \pi(a \mid s)$. Let $M := \|g\|_\infty$ and $R := \sup g - \inf g$. Under Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic}, for any initial joint distribution $\xi_0 \in \mathcal{P}(\mathcal S \times \mathcal A)$ and every $H \geq 1$,
\[
\mathbb{E}_{\xi_0}\!\left[\left(\sum_{k=0}^{H-1} g(x_k)\right)^{\!2}\right] \;\leq\; M^2\, H \;+\; 4\, M\, R\, H\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}.
\]
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
Write $S_H := \sum_{k=0}^{H-1} g(x_k)$. Expanding the square,
\[
\mathbb{E}_{\xi_0}[S_H^2] \;=\; \sum_{k=0}^{H-1} \mathbb{E}_{\xi_0}\!\left[g(x_k)^2\right] + 2 \sum_{0 \leq i < j \leq H-1} \mathbb{E}_{\xi_0}[g(x_i)\, g(x_j)].
\]
Since $|g| \leq M$, the diagonal satisfies $\sum_{k=0}^{H-1} \mathbb{E}_{\xi_0}[g(x_k)^2] \leq M^2 H$.

For the cross terms, fix $i < j$ and let $\ell := j - i \geq 1$. We bound $\mathbb{E}[g(x_\ell) \mid x_0 = (s, a)]$ uniformly over $(s, a)$. Conditional on $x_0 = (s, a)$, the law of $s_\ell$ is $P(\cdot \mid s, a)\, P_\pi^{\ell-1}$ (the first transition uses the fixed action $a$, after which $\pi$ acts). By the submultiplicative decay~\eqref{eq:tmix-decay},
\[
\bigl\|P(\cdot \mid s, a)\, P_\pi^{\ell-1} - d^\pi\bigr\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \;\leq\; 2^{-\lfloor (\ell-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}} \rfloor}.
\]
Since $a_\ell \mid s_\ell \sim \pi(\cdot \mid s_\ell)$, the law of $x_\ell = (s_\ell, a_\ell)$ satisfies the same total variation (TV) bound relative to $\nu^\pi$. Using $\mathbb{E}_{\nu^\pi}[g] = 0$ and the standard total-variation duality $|\mathbb{E}_\mu f - \mathbb{E}_{\nu^\pi} f| \leq (\sup f - \inf f)\, \|\mu - \nu^\pi\|_{\mathrm{TV}}$ with $\sup g - \inf g \leq R$:
\[
\bigl|\mathbb{E}[g(x_\ell) \mid x_0 = (s, a)]\bigr| \;\leq\; R\, 2^{-\lfloor (\ell-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}} \rfloor} \qquad \forall\, (s, a), \; \ell \geq 1.
\]
By the Markov property (tower rule over $x_i$), for $i < j$ and $\ell = j - i$:
\[
\bigl|\mathbb{E}_{\xi_0}[g(x_i)\, g(x_j)]\bigr| \;=\; \bigl|\mathbb{E}_{\xi_0}\!\bigl[g(x_i)\, \mathbb{E}[g(x_j) \mid x_i]\bigr]\bigr| \;\leq\; M\, R\, 2^{-\lfloor (j-i-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}} \rfloor}.
\]
Summing over cross terms and using the block decomposition $\sum_{m=0}^{\infty} 2^{-\lfloor m/t_{\mathrm{mix}} \rfloor} = t_{\mathrm{mix}} \sum_{q \geq 0} 2^{-q} = 2\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}$:
\[
\left|2 \sum_{0 \leq i < j \leq H-1} \mathbb{E}_{\xi_0}[g(x_i)\, g(x_j)]\right|
\;\leq\; 2 M R \sum_{\ell = 1}^{H-1} (H - \ell)\, 2^{-\lfloor (\ell-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}} \rfloor}
\;\leq\; 4 M R H\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}.
\]
Combining,
\[
\mathbb{E}_{\xi_0}[S_H^2] \;\leq\; M^2\, H \;+\; 4\, M\, R\, H\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}. \qedhere
\]
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Throughout, write $\rho := \rho(\pi)$, $Q := Q^\pi$, and $g(x) := c(x) - \rho$. Since $c(x) \in [0,1]$ and $\rho \in [0,1]$, we have $M := \|g\|_\infty \leq 1$ and $R := \mathrm{osc}(g) = \mathrm{osc}(c) \leq 1$; in particular $\mathbb{E}_{\nu^\pi}[g] = 0$, so Lemma~\ref{lem:ap-covariance-summability} applies and its conclusion $M^2 m + 4MR\, m\, t_{\mathrm{mix}} \leq m\,(1 + 4\, t_{\mathrm{mix}})$ yields, for any initial distribution $\xi_0$ and any $m \geq 1$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ap-var-bound}
\mathbb{E}_{\xi_0}\!\left[\left(\sum_{k=0}^{m-1} g(x_k)\right)^{\!2}\right] \;\leq\; m\,(1 + 4\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}).
\end{equation}

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@

  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Trajectory-level decomposition.}
Define, per trajectory $i$,
\[
U_i \;:=\; \sum_{k=0}^{\tau-1} g\bigl(x_k^{(i)}\bigr), \qquad T_i \;:=\; \sum_{k=\tau}^{H-1} g\bigl(x_k^{(i)}\bigr), \qquad n := H - \tau,
\]
and $Y_i := U_i - (\tau/n)\,T_i$. A short calculation gives
\[
\widehat Q_{H,\tau}(s,a) \;=\; \frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^B\sum_{k=0}^{H-1}\bigl(c(x_k^{(i)}) - \rho\bigr) \;-\; H\bigl(\hat\rho_{\tau, H} - \rho\bigr) \;=\; \frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^B Y_i,
\]
since $H\,(\hat\rho_{\tau, H} - \rho) = (H/n)\sum_{i}T_i/B$ and $A_i := U_i + T_i = \sum_{k=0}^{H-1} g(x_k^{(i)})$ implies $A_i - (H/n)\,T_i = U_i - (\tau/n)\,T_i = Y_i$. The $B$ trajectories are independent and identically distributed, so $\{Y_i\}$ are i.i.d.

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Variance of $Y_i$.}
By \eqref{eq:ap-var-bound} with $\xi_0 = \delta_{(s,a)}$ and horizon $\tau$:
\[
\mathbb{E}[U_i^2] \;\leq\; \tau\,(1 + 4\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}).
\]
Conditional on $x_\tau^{(i)} = (s', a')$, the chain $\{x_{\tau + j}^{(i)}\}_{j \geq 0}$ is a state--action chain under $\pi$ with initial distribution $\delta_{(s', a')}$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:ap-covariance-summability} (which handles any initial joint distribution) with horizon $n$:
\[
\mathbb{E}[T_i^2 \mid x_\tau^{(i)}] \;\leq\; n\,(1 + 4\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}) \quad\text{a.s.,}\qquad \text{hence} \qquad \mathbb{E}[T_i^2] \;\leq\; n\,(1 + 4\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}).
\]
By $(X - Y)^2 \leq 2 X^2 + 2 Y^2$:
\[
\mathrm{Var}(Y_i) \;\leq\; \mathbb{E}[Y_i^2] \;\leq\; 2\,\mathbb{E}[U_i^2] + 2\,\frac{\tau^2}{n^2}\,\mathbb{E}[T_i^2] \;\leq\; 2\,(1 + 4\, t_{\mathrm{mix}})\,\left(\tau + \frac{\tau^2}{n}\right).
\]
For $\tau = \lceil 2H/3 \rceil$ and $H \geq 4$, a direct check gives $\tau + \tau^2/n \leq 4H$ (e.g., $H = 4$: $\tau = 3, n = 1, \tau + \tau^2/n = 12 = 3H$; $H = 5$: $\tau = 4, n = 1, \tau + \tau^2/n = 20 = 4H$; asymptotically $\tau \approx 2H/3, \tau^2/n \approx 4H/3$, total $\approx 2H$). Hence, using $1 + 4\,t_{\mathrm{mix}} \leq 5\,t_{\mathrm{mix}}$ for $t_{\mathrm{mix}} \geq 1$,
\[
\mathrm{Var}(Y_i) \;\leq\; 8\,H\,(1 + 4\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}) \;\leq\; 40\,H\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}.
\]

  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Range of $Y_i$.}
Since $|g| \leq 1$: $|U_i| \leq \tau$, $|T_i| \leq n$, and $|(\tau/n)\,T_i| \leq \tau$, so $|Y_i| \leq 2\tau \leq 2H$. Hence the centered range is $|Y_i - \mathbb{E}[Y_i]| \leq 4H$.

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Bernstein concentration.}
By i.i.d.\ Bernstein's inequality, with $\sigma^2 := \mathrm{Var}(Y_i) \leq 40\,H\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}$ and range $M := 4H$, for any $u > 0$,
\[
\Pr\!\left(\left|\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^B \bigl(Y_i - \mathbb{E}[Y_i]\bigr)\right| \geq u\right) \;\leq\; 2\exp\!\left(-\frac{B\,u^2}{2\,\sigma^2 + \tfrac{2}{3}\,M\,u}\right).
\]
Setting $u = \varepsilon_Q / 2$, the right-hand side is $\leq \delta$ when
\[
B \;\geq\; \frac{4\,\log(2/\delta)}{\varepsilon_Q^2}\,\left(80\,H\, t_{\mathrm{mix}} + \tfrac{4\,H\,\varepsilon_Q}{3}\right).
\]
For $\varepsilon_Q \leq 1$ and $t_{\mathrm{mix}} \geq 1$, the variance term dominates ($80\,H\,t_{\mathrm{mix}} + \tfrac{4}{3}H\,\varepsilon_Q \leq \tfrac{244}{3}\,H\,t_{\mathrm{mix}}$), so an absolute constant $C_1 > 0$ (e.g., $C_1 = 400$) suffices:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ap-bernstein-bound}
B \;\geq\; C_1\,\frac{H\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}}{\varepsilon_Q^2}\,\log\!\frac{2}{\delta}
\qquad \Longrightarrow \qquad
\Pr\!\left(\bigl|\widehat Q_{H,\tau}(s,a) - \mathbb{E}[\widehat Q_{H,\tau}(s,a)]\bigr| \leq \varepsilon_Q/2\right) \geq 1 - \delta.
\end{equation}

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Bias bound.}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:uniform-mix}, starting from $x_0 = (s, a)$, the state marginal at time $k \geq 1$ is $P(\cdot\mid s,a)\,P_\pi^{k-1}$, and since $a_k \mid s_k \sim \pi$, the total-variation duality together with~\eqref{eq:tmix-decay} gives
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ap-reward-dev}
\bigl|\mathbb{E}[c(x_k) - \rho \mid x_0 = (s,a)]\bigr| \;\leq\; 2^{-\lfloor (k-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor}, \qquad k \geq 1,
\end{equation}
using $\mathrm{osc}(c) \leq 1$. In particular, the series $Q(s,a) = \sum_{k=0}^\infty \mathbb{E}[g(x_k) \mid x_0 = (s,a)]$ converges absolutely.

Define $q_H := \mathbb{E}[A_1 \mid x_0 = (s,a)] = \sum_{k=0}^{H-1} \mathbb{E}[g(x_k) \mid x_0]$. By~\eqref{eq:ap-reward-dev} and the block-decomposition bound $\sum_{m = m_0}^\infty 2^{-\lfloor m/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor} \leq 2\, t_{\mathrm{mix}} \cdot 2^{-\lfloor m_0/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor}$:
\[
|q_H - Q(s,a)| \;=\; \left|\sum_{k=H}^\infty \mathbb{E}[g(x_k) \mid x_0]\right| \;\leq\; \sum_{k=H}^\infty 2^{-\lfloor (k-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor} \;\leq\; 2\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}\, 2^{-\lfloor (H-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor}.
\]
Similarly, $|\mathbb{E}[T_1]| \leq 2\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}\, 2^{-\lfloor (\tau-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor}$. Using $\mathbb{E}[\widehat Q_{H,\tau}(s,a)] = \mathbb{E}[Y_1] = \mathbb{E}[A_1] - (H/n)\,\mathbb{E}[T_1]$:
\[
\bigl|\mathbb{E}[\widehat Q_{H,\tau}(s,a)] - Q(s,a)\bigr| \;\leq\; |q_H - Q(s,a)| + \frac{H}{n}\,|\mathbb{E}[T_1]|.
\]
For any $\tau$ with $n = H - \tau \geq H/5$ (e.g., $\tau = \lceil 2H/3 \rceil$ or $\tau = \lceil H/2 \rceil$) and $H \geq 4$: $H/n \leq 5$. Using $2^{-\lfloor (H-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor} \leq 2^{-\lfloor (\tau-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor}$ (since $H \geq \tau$),
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:ap-bias-bound}
\bigl|\mathbb{E}[\widehat Q_{H,\tau}(s,a)] - Q(s,a)\bigr| \;\leq\; 12\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}\, 2^{-\lfloor (\tau-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor}.
\end{equation}

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@

  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Combining.}
Choose $H$ so that $12\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}\, 2^{-\lfloor (\tau-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor} \leq \varepsilon_Q/2$, i.e., $\lfloor (\tau-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor \geq \log_2(24\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}/\varepsilon_Q)$. With $\tau \geq 2H/3$ and $H \geq 4$, this is implied by
\[
H \;\geq\; C_2\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}\, \log(C_3\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}/\varepsilon_Q)
\]
for suitable universal constants (e.g., $C_2 = 4, C_3 = 24$, which absorbs the integer-floor correction $\lfloor(\tau-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor \geq (\tau-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}} - 1$). On the high-probability event of~\eqref{eq:ap-bernstein-bound},
\begin{align*}
\bigl|\widehat Q_{H,\tau}(s,a) - Q(s,a)\bigr|
&\;\leq\; \bigl|\widehat Q_{H,\tau}(s,a) - \mathbb{E}[\widehat Q_{H,\tau}(s,a)]\bigr| + \bigl|\mathbb{E}[\widehat Q_{H,\tau}(s,a)] - Q(s,a)\bigr| \\
&\;\leq\; \varepsilon_Q/2 + \varepsilon_Q/2 \;=\; \varepsilon_Q,
\end{align*}
which holds with probability at least $1 - \delta$.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{thm:ap-pmd_sample_complexity}
Let Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic} hold, and recall the initial Lyapunov value $\Psi_0 = \Delta_0 + D_0^\star/(\eta_{-1}\bar\chi)$. Fix a target suboptimality $\varepsilon \in (0, 1)$ and a confidence $\delta \in (0, 1)$, and set $\varepsilon_Q := \varepsilon/(4(\bar\chi + 1))$ and $T := \lceil \bar\chi\,\log(2\,\Psi_0/\varepsilon) \rceil$. There exist universal constants $C_1, C_2, C_3 > 0$ such that for
\[
H \;\geq\; C_2\,t_{\mathrm{mix}}\,\log\!\frac{C_3\,t_{\mathrm{mix}}}{\varepsilon_Q},
\qquad
\tau \;:=\; \lceil 2H/3 \rceil,
\qquad
B \;\geq\; C_1\,\frac{H\,t_{\mathrm{mix}}}{\varepsilon_Q^2}\,\log\!\frac{2\,|\mathcal S|\,|\mathcal A|\,T}{\delta},
\]
the following holds. Run inexact PMD (Theorem~\ref{thm:avg-pmd-linear-inexact-limsup}) for $T$ iterations, drawing fresh independent generative-model samples at each iteration to construct $\widehat Q^t$ via Lemma~\ref{lem:ap-Q_estimation_burnin} with parameters $(H, \tau, B)$ at every $(s, a)$. Then, with probability at least $1 - \delta$,
\[
\Delta_T \;\leq\; \varepsilon.
\]
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
The proof has two ingredients: a union bound converting the per-$(s,a)$ guarantee of Lemma~\ref{lem:ap-Q_estimation_burnin} into uniform critic accuracy along the PMD trajectory, and a deterministic composition with the inexact-PMD guarantee of Theorem~\ref{thm:avg-pmd-linear-inexact-limsup}.

\@xpt\@xipt

\bf} 
{Uniform critic accuracy over $(s, a)$ and $t$.}
For each iteration $t = 0, 1, \dots, T-1$ and each $(s,a) \in \mathcal S \times \mathcal A$, the critic estimate $\widehat Q^t(s, a)$ is constructed from fresh independent generative-model samples drawn at iteration $t$. Let $\mathcal F_{t-1}$ denote the $\sigma$-algebra generated by the samples used in iterations $0, \dots, t-1$; the policy $\pi^t$ is $\mathcal F_{t-1}$-measurable, and the iteration-$t$ samples are independent of $\mathcal F_{t-1}$. Applying Lemma~\ref{lem:ap-Q_estimation_burnin} conditionally on $\mathcal F_{t-1}$ at confidence $\delta_0 := \delta/(T\,|\mathcal S|\,|\mathcal A|)$ yields, for every fixed $(s, a)$,
\[
\Pr\!\left(\bigl|\widehat Q^t(s,a) - Q^{\pi^t}(s,a)\bigr| > \varepsilon_Q \;\middle|\; \mathcal F_{t-1}\right) \;\leq\; \delta_0.
\]
A union bound over $(s,a) \in \mathcal S \times \mathcal A$ and over $t \in \{0, \dots, T-1\}$ then gives
\[
\Pr\!\left(\exists\,t,\,(s,a)\,:\,\bigl|\widehat Q^t(s,a) - Q^{\pi^t}(s,a)\bigr| > \varepsilon_Q\right) \;\leq\; T\,|\mathcal S|\,|\mathcal A|\cdot\delta_0 \;=\; \delta.
\]
On the complementary good event, Assumption~\ref{as:uniform-critic-error} holds along the realized PMD trajectory at level $\varepsilon_Q$.

  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Composition with Theorem~\ref{thm:avg-pmd-linear-inexact-limsup}.}
Condition on the good event. Since $\sup_t \chi_t \leq \bar\chi$, Theorem~\ref{thm:avg-pmd-linear-inexact-limsup} gives
\[
\bigl(\Delta_T - 2(\bar\chi + 1)\varepsilon_Q\bigr)_+ \;\leq\; \Bigl(1 - \tfrac{1}{\bar\chi}\Bigr)^T\, \Psi_0.
\]
The choice $\varepsilon_Q = \varepsilon/(4(\bar\chi + 1))$ makes the error floor equal to $\varepsilon/2$. Using $(1 - 1/\bar\chi)^T \leq \exp(-T/\bar\chi)$, the choice $T = \lceil \bar\chi\,\log(2\,\Psi_0/\varepsilon) \rceil$ ensures $(1 - 1/\bar\chi)^T\, \Psi_0 \leq \varepsilon/2$. Therefore
\[
\Delta_T \;\leq\; \tfrac{\varepsilon}{2} + \tfrac{\varepsilon}{2} \;=\; \varepsilon,
\]
with probability at least $1 - \delta$ over the sampling randomness.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{prop:ap-tabular_lower_bound}
For every $p \in (0, 1/4)$ and every $\varepsilon_Q \in (0, 1/(48 p)]$, consider the two-state, one-action MDP with $\mathcal S = \{0, 1\}$, $\mathcal A = \{a\}$, costs $c(0, a) = 0$, $c(1, a) = 1$, and transition kernel
\[
P_q \;=\; \begin{pmatrix} 1 - q & q \\ q & 1 - q \end{pmatrix}, \qquad q \in \{p,\; p + \Delta\},
\]
with $\Delta := 24\, p^2\, \varepsilon_Q$. Both instances satisfy Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic}, and the bound of Lemma~\ref{lem:uniform-mix} holds with $t_{\mathrm{mix}} = \lceil \log 2/(-\log(1-2p))\rceil = \Theta(1/p)$, and their differential $Q$-functions $Q_p, Q_{p+\Delta}$ (defined via the centered Poisson convention $\sum_s d(s)\,h(s) = 0$) satisfy $\|Q_p - Q_{p + \Delta}\|_\infty \geq 4\,\varepsilon_Q$. Any (possibly adaptive) generative-model algorithm that outputs $\widehat Q \in \mathbb R^2$ with
\[
\Pr\!\left(\bigl\|\widehat Q - Q_q\bigr\|_\infty \leq \varepsilon_Q\right) \;\geq\; \tfrac{2}{3} \qquad \text{for both } q \in \{p,\; p + \Delta\}
\]
must use at least $N \geq 1 / (5184\, p^3\, \varepsilon_Q^2) = \Omega(t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3 / \varepsilon_Q^2)$ generative transition queries.
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
The proof proceeds in four steps: verify the mixing and Poisson-solution arithmetic, compute the differential-$Q$ gap between the two instances, bound the per-query KL divergence, and apply Le Cam's two-point testing reduction.

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Step 1: Mixing and Poisson solution.}
For $q \in (0, 1/2)$, the kernel $P_q$ has eigenvalues $1$ and $1 - 2q$. Hence for any initial distribution $\mu_0 \in \mathcal P(\{0, 1\})$ and any $k \geq 0$,
\[
\bigl\|P_q^k\, \mu_0 - d\bigr\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \;\leq\; \tfrac{1}{2}\,(1 - 2q)^k,
\]
where $d = (1/2,\, 1/2)$ is the stationary distribution. At $k = t_{\mathrm{mix}}(q) := \lceil \log 2/(-\log(1-2q))\rceil$, the right-hand side is at most $1/4$, so the bound of Lemma~\ref{lem:uniform-mix} holds with this $t_{\mathrm{mix}}$. Since $-\log(1-2q) = 2q\,(1 + O(q))$ for $q \in (0, 1/4]$, we have $t_{\mathrm{mix}}(q) = \Theta(1/q)$.

Solving the Poisson equation $\rho + h = c + P_q\, h$ with $\rho = 1/2$ (the average cost) and the centering $\sum_s d(s)\,h(s) = 0$:
\[
h_q(0) = -\frac{1}{4q}, \qquad h_q(1) = \frac{1}{4q}.
\]
Since $|\mathcal A| = 1$, the differential $Q$-function coincides with $h$:
\[
Q_q(0, a) = h_q(0) = -\frac{1}{4q}, \qquad Q_q(1, a) = h_q(1) = \frac{1}{4q}.
\]

 
  \@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@} 
                {1.5ex \@plus 0.5ex \@minus 0.2ex} 
                {-1em} 
                {

  \@setfontsize\normalsize\@xpt\@xipt

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Step 2: Differential-$Q$ gap.}
Fix $q_0 = p$ and $q_1 = p + \Delta$ with $\Delta \in (0, p/2]$. Then
\[
\bigl\|Q_{q_0} - Q_{q_1}\bigr\|_\infty \;=\; \left|\frac{1}{4p} - \frac{1}{4(p + \Delta)}\right| \;=\; \frac{\Delta}{4p(p + \Delta)}.
\]
Since $\Delta \leq p/2$, $p + \Delta \leq 3p/2$, so $4p(p + \Delta) \leq 6p^2$ and
\[
\bigl\|Q_{q_0} - Q_{q_1}\bigr\|_\infty \;\geq\; \frac{\Delta}{6p^2}.
\]
Setting $\Delta = 24\, p^2\, \varepsilon_Q$ yields $\|Q_{q_0} - Q_{q_1}\|_\infty \geq 4\,\varepsilon_Q$. The regime constraint $\Delta \leq p/2$ becomes $\varepsilon_Q \leq 1/(48 p)$.

 
  \@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@} 
                {1.5ex \@plus 0.5ex \@minus 0.2ex} 
                {-1em} 
                {

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@

  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Step 3: Per-query KL bound.}
Each generative query returns a Bernoulli sample: from either state, the probability of transitioning to the opposite state is $q$ (the role of ``opposite state'' is swapped between queries from states $0$ and $1$, but the KL divergence is invariant under this relabeling). Hence every query is a $\mathrm{Bern}(q)$ sample, and the KL divergence between the two instances' per-query laws is
\[
\mathrm{kl}\bigl(\mathrm{Bern}(p) \,\bigl\|\, \mathrm{Bern}(p + \Delta)\bigr) \;=\; \int_p^{p + \Delta} \frac{u - p}{u\,(1 - u)}\, du.
\]
For $u \in [p, p + \Delta]$ with $p \leq 1/4$ and $\Delta \leq p/2$: $u \geq p$ and $1 - u \geq 1 - p - \Delta \geq 5/8$, hence $u(1 - u) \geq 5p/8$ and
\[
\mathrm{kl}\bigl(\mathrm{Bern}(p) \,\bigl\|\, \mathrm{Bern}(p + \Delta)\bigr) \;\leq\; \frac{8}{5p} \cdot \frac{\Delta^2}{2} \;=\; \frac{4\,\Delta^2}{5\,p} \;\leq\; \frac{2\,\Delta^2}{p}.
\]
Substituting $\Delta = 24\, p^2\, \varepsilon_Q$:
\[
\mathrm{kl}\bigl(\mathrm{Bern}(p) \,\bigl\|\, \mathrm{Bern}(p + \Delta)\bigr) \;\leq\; 1152\, p^3\, \varepsilon_Q^2.
\]

 
  \@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@} 
                {1.5ex \@plus 0.5ex \@minus 0.2ex} 
                {-1em} 
                { 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize\normalsize\@xpt\@xipt

  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@

\@xpt\@xipt

  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Step 4: Le Cam's two-point reduction.}
Let $\mathbb{P}_0, \mathbb{P}_1$ denote the joint distributions over the algorithm's queries, observations, and output under $M_{p}$ and $M_{p + \Delta}$, respectively. Since every per-query conditional KL is bounded by $1152\, p^3\, \varepsilon_Q^2$ uniformly over the queried state, the KL chain rule for adaptive samplers gives
\[
\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_0 \,\|\, \mathbb{P}_1) \;\leq\; N \cdot 1152\, p^3\, \varepsilon_Q^2.
\]

Consider the test $\psi(\widehat Q) := \arg\min_{j \in \{0, 1\}} \|\widehat Q - Q_{q_j}\|_\infty$. Since $\|Q_{q_0} - Q_{q_1}\|_\infty \geq 4\,\varepsilon_Q$, the event $\{\|\widehat Q - Q_{q_0}\|_\infty \leq \varepsilon_Q\}$ implies $\psi = 0$, and similarly under $M_{q_1}$. Hence, under the hypothesis of the proposition,
\[
\Pr_{M_{q_j}}(\psi \neq j) \;\leq\; 1/3, \qquad j = 0, 1.
\]
Le Cam's inequality $\inf_\psi \max_j \Pr_{M_{q_j}}(\psi \neq j) \geq \tfrac{1}{2}(1 - \|\mathbb{P}_0 - \mathbb{P}_1\|_{\mathrm{TV}})$ then gives $\|\mathbb{P}_0 - \mathbb{P}_1\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \geq 1/3$. Combined with Pinsker's inequality $\|\mathbb{P}_0 - \mathbb{P}_1\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \leq \sqrt{\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_0 \| \mathbb{P}_1)/2}$, we obtain
\[
\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_0 \,\|\, \mathbb{P}_1) \;\geq\; \frac{2}{9}.
\]
Substituting into the KL upper bound:
\[
N \cdot 1152\, p^3\, \varepsilon_Q^2 \;\geq\; \frac{2}{9} \qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad N \;\geq\; \frac{1}{5184\, p^3\, \varepsilon_Q^2}.
\]
Since $t_{\mathrm{mix}} = \Theta(1/p)$, this is $N = \Omega(t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3 / \varepsilon_Q^2)$.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:as-non-tabular-linear-convergence}
Assume Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic} and let $\{\pi^t\}_{t\ge 0}$ be generated by \eqref{eq:pmd-update} (with $\tilde Q^t(s,a) = \phi_{s,a}^\top w_t$). Suppose Assumptions~\ref{as:approx-error}--\ref{as:L2-mismatch-bound} hold and define
\[
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{floor}} \;:=\; 2\Big(\bar\chi\sqrt{C_{+}}+\sqrt{C_{\star}}\Big)
\Big(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}}\Big).
\]
If there exists $\bar\chi\in(1,\infty)$ with $\sup_t \chi_t \leq \bar\chi$ and the step sizes satisfy $\eta_t \ge\frac{\bar\chi}{\bar\chi-1}\,\eta_{t-1}$ for all $t\ge 0$ with some $\eta_{-1}>0$, then for all $t\ge 0$,
\[
(\mathbb{E}[\Delta_t]-\varepsilon_{\mathrm{floor}})_+
\le
\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{\bar\chi}\Bigr)^t\,\left(\mathbb{E}[\Delta_0]+\frac{1}{\eta_{-1}\,\bar\chi}\,\mathbb{E}[D_0^\star]\right).
\]
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Non-tabular PMD is the update~\eqref{eq:pmd-update} with the LFA critic $\tilde Q^t(s,a) = \phi_{s,a}^\top w_t$. We apply Lemma~\ref{lem:master} after bounding the critic-error quantities $A_t, B_t$ in expectation.

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Bounding $A_t$ and $B_t$ via state--action concentrability.}
Define the scalar error function $e_t(s,a) := w_t^\top \phi_{s,a} - Q^{\pi^t}(s,a)$. Expanding the inner products against $\pi^{t+1} - \pi^t$ and $\pi^t - \pi^\star$ into expectations over state--action pairs,
\[
A_t = \Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t+1}}[e_t] - \mathbb{E}_{d^{t+1}\times\pi^t}[e_t]\Bigr|, \qquad B_t = \Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{d^\star\times\pi^t}[e_t] - \mathbb{E}_{\nu^\star}[e_t]\Bigr|,
\]
where $\nu^t := d^t \times \pi^t$ denotes the stationary state--action distribution of $\pi^t$.

We use the generic change-of-measure bound: for any two distributions $\mu,\nu$ on $\mathcal S\times\mathcal A$
with $\nu \ll \mu$ and any measurable $f$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:com_bound}
\big|\mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim \nu}[f(s,a)]\big|
\;\le\;
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim \mu}\!\left[\left(\frac{\nu(s,a)}{\mu(s,a)}\right)^2\right]}\;
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim \mu}[f(s,a)^2]}.
\end{equation}

First, apply \eqref{eq:com_bound} with $\mu=\nu^t$ and $\nu=\nu^{t+1}$, and use
Assumption~\ref{as:L2-mismatch-bound} to get
\[
\big|\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t+1}}[e_t]\big|
\;\le\;
\sqrt{C_+}\,\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^t}[e_t^2]}.
\]
Next, note that the mixed distribution $d^{t+1}\times \pi^t(\cdot\mid s)$ satisfies
\[
\frac{d^{t+1}(s)\pi^t(a\mid s)}{\nu^t(s,a)}
=
\frac{d^{t+1}(s)}{d^t(s)}
=
\sum_{a'} \pi^t(a'\mid s)\,\frac{\nu^{t+1}(s,a')}{\nu^t(s,a')},
\]
so by Jensen's inequality,
\[
\left(\frac{d^{t+1}(s)}{d^t(s)}\right)^2
\le
\sum_{a'} \pi^t(a'\mid s)\left(\frac{\nu^{t+1}(s,a')}{\nu^t(s,a')}\right)^2.
\]
Taking expectation over $s\sim d^t$ yields
\[
\mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim \nu^t}\!\left[\left(\frac{d^{t+1}(s)\pi^t(a\mid s)}{\nu^t(s,a)}\right)^2\right]
=
\mathbb{E}_{s\sim d^t}\!\left[\left(\frac{d^{t+1}(s)}{d^t(s)}\right)^2\right]
\le
\mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim \nu^t}\!\left[\left(\frac{\nu^{t+1}(s,a)}{\nu^t(s,a)}\right)^2\right]
\le C_+.
\]
Applying \eqref{eq:com_bound} with $\mu=\nu^t$ and $\nu=d^{t+1}\times \pi^t(\cdot\mid s)$ gives
\[
\big|\mathbb{E}_{d^{t+1}\times \pi^t}[e_t]\big|
\;\le\;
\sqrt{C_+}\,\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^t}[e_t^2]}.
\]
Therefore,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:E1_bound_sa}
A_t \le 2\sqrt{C_+}\,\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^t}[e_t^2]}.
\end{equation}
An analogous argument using Assumption~\ref{as:L2-mismatch-bound} (with $\nu^\star$ in place of $\nu^{t+1}$) yields
\begin{equation}\label{eq:E2_bound_sa}
B_t \le 2\sqrt{C_\star}\,\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^t}[e_t^2]}.
\end{equation}

Finally, decompose
\[
e_t(s,a)=\phi_{s,a}^\top(w_t-w_t^\star) + \big(\phi_{s,a}^\top w_t^\star - Q^{\pi^t}(s,a)\big).
\]
By the triangle inequality in $L_2(\nu^t)$,
\begin{align}
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^t}[e_t^2]}
\;&\le\;
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^t}\!\left[\big(\phi_{s,a}^\top(w_t-w_t^\star)\big)^2\right]}
+
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^t}\!\left[\big(\phi_{s,a}^\top w_t^\star - Q^{\pi^t}(s,a)\big)^2\right]} \\
&\leq
\|w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}
+
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}},
\end{align}
where the second inequality follows from Assumption~\ref{as:approx-error}. Taking expectations yields
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:e2_bound_sa}
\mathbb{E}\left[\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^t}[e_t^2]}\right] \leq
\mathbb{E}[\|w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}]
+
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}}
\leq
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\|w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}^2]}
+
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}}
\leq
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}
+
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}},
\end{equation}
where the last inequality follows from Assumption~\ref{as:stat-error}.

  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Applying the master lemma in expectation.}
Combining \eqref{eq:E1_bound_sa}, \eqref{eq:E2_bound_sa}, and \eqref{eq:e2_bound_sa} yields
\[
\mathbb{E}[A_t] \leq 2\sqrt{C_+}\bigl(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}} + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}}\bigr), \qquad \mathbb{E}[B_t] \leq 2\sqrt{C_\star}\bigl(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}} + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}}\bigr).
\]
Taking expectations in the one-step recursion underlying Lemma~\ref{lem:master}(i) and iterating, with $\bar A = 2\sqrt{C_+}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}} + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}})$ and $\bar B = 2\sqrt{C_\star}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}} + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}})$, gives the error floor $\bar\chi\bar A + \bar B = 2(\bar\chi\sqrt{C_+} + \sqrt{C_\star})(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}} + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}}) = \varepsilon_{\mathrm{floor}}$, yielding the claimed bound.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{cor:avg_reward_qnpg_sample_complexity}
Suppose Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic} holds. Fix $t$ and consider the regression target
\[
w_t^\star \in \arg\min_{w\in\mathbb{R}^d}\;
\mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim \nu^t}\bigl[(w^\top\phi_{s,a} - Q^t(s,a))^2\bigr]
\]
with $|Q^t(s,a)| \leq G$. Additionally suppose that:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] (Bounded features) $\|\phi_{s,a}\|_2\le L$ for all $(s,a)$.
\item[(ii)] (Uniform covariance lower bound) For all $t$,
$\Sigma_t := \mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim \nu^{t}}\bigl[\phi_{s,a}\phi_{s,a}^\top\bigr]
\succeq \mu I_d$ for some $\mu>0$.
\item[(iii)] (Unbiased label oracle) The label oracle returns $\widehat Q^t(s,a)$ with
\[
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\widehat Q^{t}(s,a)\mid s,a\right]=Q^{t}(s,a),
\qquad
\mathbb{E}\!\left[(\widehat Q^{t}(s,a))^2\mid s,a\right]\le G^2.
\]
\end{enumerate}

At each outer iteration $t$, compute $w_t$ by running $K$ steps of averaged SGD on the quadratic loss
\[
f_t(w)
:=
\frac12\,
\mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim \nu^{t}}
\bigl[(w^\top \phi_{s,a}-Q^t(s,a))^2\bigr],
\]
with independent and identically distributed labeled pairs $(s,a,\widehat Q^t(s,a))$, where $(s,a)\sim\nu^t$ and the label satisfies (iii), constant step size $\alpha=\frac{1}{4L^2}$, initialization $w_{t,0}=0$, and output
$w_t:=\frac{1}{K+1}\sum_{k=0}^K w_{t,k}$.

Then, the SGD output satisfies the statistical-error bound
\[
\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}^2\bigr]
\;\le\;
\frac{4}{K}
\Bigl(
\frac{L^2G}{\mu}\,(\sqrt{2d}+1)+G\sqrt{2d}
\Bigr)^2.
\]
Consequently, for any desired level $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}>0$, it suffices to choose
\[
K \;\ge\; \frac{4}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2}
\Bigl(
\frac{L^2G}{\mu}\,(\sqrt{2d}+1)+G\sqrt{2d}
\Bigr)^2
\]
to guarantee $\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}^2\bigr]\le \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2$.
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Write $\pi=\pi^t$. Define $x:=\phi_{s,a}\in\mathbb{R}^d$ and $y:=\widehat Q^t(s,a)\in\mathbb{R}$ for $(s,a)\sim\nu^t$,
and set $z:=yx$.

Up to an additive constant independent of $w$, the loss $f_t$ equals
\[
f_t(w)
=
\frac12\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[(w^\top x)^2-2w^\top z\bigr]+\mathrm{const},
\]
which matches the quadratic form of \citet[Theorem~1]{bach_non-strongly-convex_2013} (we follow the restatement in \citet[Theorem~8]{yuan_linear_2023} for convenience of notation). Denote a minimizer by $w^\star=w_t^\star$. The SGD update is $w_{k+1}=w_k-\alpha (w_k^\top x-y)x$.

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Verify the Bach--Moulines conditions.}
Condition (i) is satisfied with $m=d$, condition (ii) holds because the labeled pairs are independent and identically distributed and condition (iii) is satisfied by our assumption (iii).

Now define the residual $\xi:=z-\langle w^\star,x\rangle x=(y-w^{\star\top}x)x$. Since $w^\star$ is a minimizer and $\nabla f_t(w)=\mathbb{E}\bigl[(w^\top x-y)x\bigr]$, first-order optimality gives $\mathbb{E}[\xi]=-\nabla f_t(w^\star) = 0$, hence condition (iv) is satisfied.

Since $\|x\|\le L$ a.s., we have
\[
\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|x\|^2xx^\top\bigr]\preceq L^2\,\mathbb{E}[xx^\top],
\]
so condition (vi) holds with $R=L$, and the theorem uses the constant step size
$\eta=\frac{1}{4R^2}=\frac{1}{4L^2}$, which matches our choice $\alpha=\frac{1}{4L^2}$.

Let $\Sigma_t:=\mathbb{E}[xx^\top]$; by assumption $\Sigma_t\succeq\mu I_d$.
Using $|Q^\pi(s,a)|\le G$ and $\|x\|\le L$,
\[
\|w^\star\|
=
\|\Sigma_t^{-1}\mathbb{E}[Q^\pi(s,a)x]\|
\le \|\Sigma_t^{-1}\|\,\mathbb{E}[|Q^\pi(s,a)|\,\|x\|]
\le \frac{LG}{\mu}.
\]

Now define the residual $\xi:=z-\langle w^\star,x\rangle x=(y-w^{\star\top}x)x$.
Using $\mathbb{E}[y^2\mid x]\le G^2$ and $(w^{\star\top}x)^2\le \|w^\star\|^2\|x\|^2\le L^2\|w^\star\|^2$,
\[
\mathbb{E}\bigl[(y-w^{\star\top}x)^2\mid x\bigr]
\le 2\mathbb{E}[y^2\mid x]+2(w^{\star\top}x)^2
\le 2G^2+2L^2\|w^\star\|^2
\le 2G^2\Bigl(1+\frac{L^2}{\mu}\Bigr)^2.
\]
Therefore
\[
\mathbb{E}[\xi\xi^\top]
=
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathbb{E}\bigl[(y-w^{\star\top}x)^2\mid x\bigr]\,xx^\top\right]
\preceq
\sigma^2\,\mathbb{E}[xx^\top],
\qquad
\sigma:=\sqrt{2}\,G\Bigl(1+\frac{L^2}{\mu}\Bigr).
\]

\bf} 
{Apply Bach--Moulines} Applying \citet[Theorem~1]{bach_non-strongly-convex_2013} (in the form of \citet[Theorem~8]{yuan_linear_2023}, with $m=d$, $n=K$, $\theta_0=0$) yields
\[
\mathbb{E}\bigl[f_t(w_t)-f_t(w^\star)\bigr]
\le
\frac{2}{K}\Bigl(\sigma\sqrt{d}+L\|w^\star\|\Bigr)^2.
\]
Since $f_t$ is quadratic with Hessian $\Sigma_t=\mathbb{E}[xx^\top]$,
\[
f_t(w)-f_t(w^\star)=\frac12\|w-w^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}^2,
\]
hence
\[
\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|w_t-w^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}^2\bigr]
\le
\frac{4}{K}\Bigl(\sigma\sqrt{d}+L\|w^\star\|\Bigr)^2
\le
\frac{4}{K}
\Bigl(
G\sqrt{2d}+\frac{L^2G}{\mu}(\sqrt{2d}+1)
\Bigr)^2,
\]
where we used $\sigma\sqrt{d}=G(1+L^2/\mu)\sqrt{2d}$ and $L\|w^\star\|\le L^2G/\mu$.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:ap-w-sample-complexity}
Suppose $\|\phi_{s,a}\|_2\le L$ for all $(s,a)$ and for all $t$, $\Sigma_t := \mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim \nu^{t}}\bigl[\phi_{s,a}\phi_{s,a}^\top\bigr] \succeq \mu I_d$
for some $\mu>0$. In addition, suppose Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic} holds. Fix an outer iteration $t$ and let $w_t$ be the output of Algorithm~\ref{alg:end2end_w_oracle} with step size
$\alpha=\frac{1}{4L^2}$. Let $\mathcal F_{t-1}$ denote the $\sigma$-algebra generated by the trajectory up to the start of this critic call; the policy $\pi^t$ and the current state of the trajectory are $\mathcal F_{t-1}$-measurable.

Then for any target critic accuracy $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}\in(0,1)$, the integer parameter choices, with $H\ge 4$,
\[
H \;=\; \Theta\!\left(t_{\mathrm{mix}}\,\log\!\frac{2t_{\mathrm{mix}}}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}}\right),
\qquad
\tau \;=\; \left\lceil\frac{H}{2}\right\rceil,
\qquad
B \;=\; 1,
\]
\[
m \;=\; \Theta\!\left(t_{\mathrm{mix}}\,\log\!\frac{t_{\mathrm{mix}}\,(1+L^2/\mu)}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}}\right),
\]
\[
K \;=\; \Theta\!\left(
\frac{t_{\mathrm{mix}}^2\,\log(2t_{\mathrm{mix}}/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}})\,d\,(1+L^2/\mu)^2}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2}
\right)
\]
suffice to guarantee
\[
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}^2 \,\middle|\, \mathcal F_{t-1}\right] \;\le\; \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2 \quad\text{almost surely,}\qquad\text{hence}\qquad \mathbb{E}\!\left[\|w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}^2\right] \;\le\; \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2.
\]

Moreover, Algorithm~\ref{alg:end2end_w_oracle} uses at most $K(m+BH)$ transitions, so
under the above choices (with $BH = H = \widetilde O(t_{\mathrm{mix}})$) the total number of transitions is
\[
K(m+BH)
\;=\;
\widetilde{O}\!\left(
\frac{t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3\,d\,(1+L^2/\mu)^2}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2}
\right),
\]
where $\widetilde{O}(\cdot)$ hides absolute constants and polylogarithmic factors in
$t_{\mathrm{mix}},L,\mu,1/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}$.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Fix $t$.

\bf} 
{Coupling with independent pairs.}
Throughout, we condition on $\mathcal F_{t-1}$, which fixes $\pi^t$ and the current state of the trajectory. Write $Z_k := (s_k, a_k, \widehat Q_k)$ for the $k$-th pair of Algorithm~\ref{alg:end2end_w_oracle} and its label, so that $w_t$ is a function of $Z_1,\dots,Z_K$. Let $Z_1',\dots,Z_K'$ be independent, where $Z_k' := (s_k', a_k', \widehat Q_k')$ has $(s_k', a_k') \sim \nu^t$ and a label $\widehat Q_k'$ computed in the same way from a rollout that starts at $(s_k', a_k')$; this is the sampling of Lemma~\ref{cor:avg_reward_qnpg_sample_complexity}. Conditional on the trajectory up to the start of the $k$-th burn-in, the law of $s_k$ is $P_{\pi^t}^m(\cdot\mid s_{k,0})$, which by~\eqref{eq:tmix-decay} is within $\delta_m := 2^{-\lfloor m/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor}$ of $d^{\pi^t}$ in total variation. Since $a_k \mid s_k \sim \pi^t(\cdot\mid s_k)$ and $\widehat Q_k$ is computed from the rollout that starts at $(s_k, a_k)$, the law of $Z_k$ satisfies the same TV bound relative to the law of $Z_k'$, and, by convexity of the total variation distance, so does its conditional law given only $Z_1,\dots,Z_{k-1}$, which averages these laws. Now replace $Z_K, Z_{K-1},\dots,Z_1$ in turn by independent draws distributed as $Z_K', Z_{K-1}',\dots,Z_1'$. The step that replaces $Z_k$ changes only the conditional law of the $k$-th coordinate given the first $k-1$, from that of $Z_k$ to the law of $Z_k'$, so the joint law moves in total variation by the average distance between these two laws, which is at most $\delta_m$. Hence the joint laws $\mathbb{P}$ of $(Z_1,\dots,Z_K)$ and $\mathbb{P}'$ of $(Z_1',\dots,Z_K')$ satisfy $\|\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{P}'\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le K\delta_m$. Since the $Z_k$ take finitely many values, an optimal coupling of $\mathbb{P}$ and $\mathbb{P}'$ \citep[Proposition~4.7 and Remark~4.8]{levin_markov_2017} lets us realize $Z_1',\dots,Z_K'$ on an extended probability space so that $Z_k'\neq Z_k$ for some $k$ with probability at most $K\delta_m$. Let $w_t'$ be the output of the same SGD run on $Z_1',\dots,Z_K'$; it equals $w_t$ whenever $Z_k'=Z_k$ for all $k$.

  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Almost-sure bound on $\|w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}$.}
With $B=1$, each label equals the quantity $Y_1$ in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:ap-Q_estimation_burnin}, so $|\widehat Q_k|\le 2H$ by the range bound there. Each SGD step is $w_{t,k}=(I_d-\alpha\phi\phi^\top)w_{t,k-1}+\alpha\widehat Q_k\phi$ with $\phi=\phi_{s_k,a_k}$, and $\|I_d-\alpha\phi\phi^\top\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le 1$ since $\alpha\|\phi\|_2^2\le 1/4$, so $\|w_{t,k}\|_2\le\|w_{t,k-1}\|_2+2\alpha LH=\|w_{t,k-1}\|_2+H/(2L)$. Hence $\|w_{t,k}\|_2\le kH/(2L)$ and, averaging, $\|w_t\|_2\le KH/(4L)$. Moreover, $\|w_t^\star\|_2\le L\,\|Q^{\pi^t}\|_\infty/\mu$ by the bound on $\|w^\star\|$ in the proof of Lemma~\ref{cor:avg_reward_qnpg_sample_complexity}. For the Poisson bound $\|Q^{\pi^t}\|_\infty\le 1+2\,t_{\mathrm{mix}}$, the series for $Q^{\pi^t}$ in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:ap-Q_estimation_burnin} has a first term of absolute value at most $1$, and by~\eqref{eq:ap-reward-dev} the absolute values of its remaining terms sum to at most $\sum_{\ell\ge0}2^{-\lfloor \ell/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor}=2\,t_{\mathrm{mix}}$. Since $\Sigma_t\preceq L^2I_d$, almost surely
\[
\|w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}
\;\le\;
L\bigl(\|w_t\|_2+\|w_t^\star\|_2\bigr)
\;\le\;
\frac{KH}{4}+\frac{(1+2\,t_{\mathrm{mix}})L^2}{\mu}.
\]

\@xpt\@xipt

  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Decomposition.}
Define the conditional-mean label
\[
\widehat Q(s,a) \;:=\; \mathbb{E}\!\left[\widehat Q_{H,\tau}^{\pi^t}(s,a)\mid s,a\right],
\]
and the population minimizer
\[
\tilde w_t \in \arg\min_w \mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim\nu^t}\!\left[(w^\top\phi_{s,a}-\widehat Q(s,a))^2\right].
\]
Split the expectation according to whether $Z_k'=Z_k$ for all $k$. If so, $w_t=w_t'$, and we write $w_t'-w_t^\star=(w_t'-\tilde w_t)+(\tilde w_t-w_t^\star)$ and use $\|x+y\|_{\Sigma_t}^2\le 2\|x\|_{\Sigma_t}^2+2\|y\|_{\Sigma_t}^2$; if not, which has probability at most $K\delta_m$, we use the almost-sure bound. This gives
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:three_term_decomp}
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}^2\right]
\le
2\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|w_t'-\tilde w_t\|_{\Sigma_t}^2\right]
+2\,\|\tilde w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}^2
+K\delta_m\Bigl(\frac{KH}{4}+\frac{(1+2\,t_{\mathrm{mix}})L^2}{\mu}\Bigr)^2.
\end{equation}

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Bias term $\|\tilde w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}^2$.}
By first-order optimality for the two population least-squares problems under $\nu^t$ with
targets $Q^{\pi^t}$ and $\widehat Q$, we have the standard inequality
\[
\|\tilde w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}
\le
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{(s,a)\sim\nu^t}\!\left[(\widehat Q(s,a)-Q^{\pi^t}(s,a))^2\right]}
\le
\|\widehat Q-Q^{\pi^t}\|_\infty.
\]
By the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:ap-Q_estimation_burnin} (bias bound~\eqref{eq:ap-bias-bound}),
\[
\|\widehat Q-Q^{\pi^t}\|_\infty
\;\le\;
12\,t_{\mathrm{mix}}\,2^{-\lfloor (\tau-1)/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor}.
\]
With the choice $\tau=\lceil H/2\rceil$ and $H=\Theta(t_{\mathrm{mix}}\,\log(2t_{\mathrm{mix}}/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}))$,
the right-hand side is $O(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}})$, hence
$\|\tilde w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}^2 \le \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2/9$ for a suitable constant in the
$\Theta(\cdot)$ choice of $H$.

  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{SGD term $\mathbb{E}[\|w_t'-\tilde w_t\|_{\Sigma_t}^2]$.}
By definition of $\widehat Q$, the labels of Algorithm~\ref{alg:end2end_w_oracle} ($B = 1$) satisfy $\mathbb{E}[\widehat Q_{H,\tau}^{\pi^t}(s,a)\mid s,a]=\widehat Q(s,a)$. The Bach--Moulines SGD rate depends on the label second moment, which we bound directly (rather than via the high-probability guarantee of Lemma~\ref{lem:Q_estimation_burnin}):
\begin{align*}
\mathbb{E}[(\widehat Q_{H,\tau}^{\pi^t}(s,a))^2\mid s,a]
&\;\leq\;
\|\widehat Q\|_\infty^2 + \mathrm{Var}(\widehat Q_{H,\tau}^{\pi^t}(s,a)\mid s,a)
\;=\;
O(t_{\mathrm{mix}}^2) + O(H\,t_{\mathrm{mix}})\\
&\;=\;
O\bigl(t_{\mathrm{mix}}^2\log(2t_{\mathrm{mix}}/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}})\bigr),
\end{align*}
where the last step uses $H=\Theta(t_{\mathrm{mix}}\log(2t_{\mathrm{mix}}/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}))$, the mean bound uses the bias and Poisson bounds above, and the variance bound follows from $\mathbb{E}[Y_i^2]\le 2(1+4\,t_{\mathrm{mix}})(\tau+\tau^2/n)$ in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:ap-Q_estimation_burnin}, with $\tau=\lceil H/2\rceil$. Therefore, Lemma~\ref{cor:avg_reward_qnpg_sample_complexity} applies to $Z_1',\dots,Z_K'$, with target $\widehat Q$ in place of $Q^t$ (so that $w_t^\star$ there is $\tilde w_t$, and $|\widehat Q|\le G$ by Jensen's inequality) and label second moment $G^2=O(t_{\mathrm{mix}}^2\log(2t_{\mathrm{mix}}/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}))$, and yields
\[
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\|w_t'-\tilde w_t\|_{\Sigma_t}^2\right]
=
O\!\left(\frac{t_{\mathrm{mix}}^2\log(2t_{\mathrm{mix}}/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}})\,d\,(1+L^2/\mu)^2}{K}\right).
\]
The stated choice
$K=\Theta\!\big(\frac{t_{\mathrm{mix}}^2\log(2t_{\mathrm{mix}}/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}})\, d (1+L^2/\mu)^2}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2}\big)$ ensures
$\mathbb{E}[\|w_t'-\tilde w_t\|_{\Sigma_t}^2]\le \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2/9$.

\bf} 
{Burn-in term.}
Since $K$ and $H$ are bounded by polynomials in $t_{\mathrm{mix}}$, $1+L^2/\mu$ and $1/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}$ (using $d\le L^2/\mu$, from $d\mu\le\mathrm{tr}(\Sigma_t)\le L^2$), a suitable constant in the $\Theta(\cdot)$ choice of $m$ gives $m\ge t_{\mathrm{mix}}\bigl\lceil\log_2\!\bigl(2K(KH/4+(1+2\,t_{\mathrm{mix}})L^2/\mu)^2/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2\bigr)\bigr\rceil$. Then $\delta_m=2^{-\lfloor m/t_{\mathrm{mix}}\rfloor}$ makes the last term of~\eqref{eq:three_term_decomp} at most $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2/2$.

  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Sample complexity.}
Plugging the three bounds into \eqref{eq:three_term_decomp} gives
$\mathbb{E}[\|w_t-w_t^\star\|_{\Sigma_t}^2]\le 4\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2/9+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2/2<\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2$, conditionally on $\mathcal F_{t-1}$; taking expectations gives the unconditional bound.
Finally, each SGD step uses $m$ transitions for its burn-in and $H$ transitions for its rollout ($B = 1$), so the per-step cost is $m + H = \widetilde O(t_{\mathrm{mix}})$ and the total number of transitions is $K(m+BH) = \widetilde O(t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3\,d\,(1+L^2/\mu)^2/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2)$.

<!-- theorem-8: body_md -->
\label{thm:ap-non_tabular_pmd_sample_complexity_generative}
Fix target accuracy $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$ and assume the setting of
Theorem~\ref{thm:non-tabular-linear-convergence} and Lemma~\ref{lem:w-sample-complexity}. Run the update \eqref{eq:pmd-update} (with $\tilde Q^t(s,a) = \phi_{s,a}^\top w_t$) for $T$ steps with $w_t$ estimated from Algorithm~\ref{alg:end2end_w_oracle} and assume that
\[
\varepsilon \;\ge\; 8\big(\bar\chi\sqrt{C_{+}}+\sqrt{C_{\star}}\big)\varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}}.
\]
Pick Algorithm~\ref{alg:end2end_w_oracle} parameters as
\[
H \;=\; \Theta\!\left(t_{\mathrm{mix}}\,\log\!\frac{(\bar\chi\sqrt{C_{+}}+\sqrt{C_{\star}})\,t_{\mathrm{mix}}}{\varepsilon}\right),
\qquad
\tau=\left\lceil\frac{H}{2}\right\rceil,
\qquad
B=1,
\]
\[
m \;=\; \Theta\!\left(t_{\mathrm{mix}}\,\log\!\frac{(\bar\chi\sqrt{C_{+}}+\sqrt{C_{\star}})\,t_{\mathrm{mix}}\,(1+L^2/\mu)}{\varepsilon}\right),
\]
\[
K \;=\; \Theta\!\left(
\frac{t_{\mathrm{mix}}^2\,\log\bigl((\bar\chi\sqrt{C_{+}}+\sqrt{C_{\star}})\,t_{\mathrm{mix}}/\varepsilon\bigr)\,d\,(1+L^2/\mu)^2\,(\bar\chi\sqrt{C_{+}}+\sqrt{C_{\star}})^2}{\varepsilon^{2}}
\right).
\]
Then, $T \ge \left\lceil \bar\chi\log\!\Bigl(\frac{2\,\mathbb{E}[\Psi_0]}{\varepsilon}\Bigr)\right\rceil$ implies $\mathbb{E}[\Delta_T]\le\varepsilon$, where $\Psi_0 = \Delta_0 + D_0^\star/(\eta_{-1}\bar\chi)$ is the initial Lyapunov value.

Moreover, each outer iteration uses at most $K(m+BH)$ transitions, hence the total
number of transitions over $T$ outer iterations is at most $T\,K(m+BH)$, and with
$T=\left\lceil \bar\chi\log\!\bigl(\frac{2\,\mathbb{E}[\Psi_0]}{\varepsilon}\bigr)\right\rceil$ and the above choices this is
\[
T\,K(m+BH)
\;=\;
\widetilde{O}\!\left(
\frac{
t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3\,d\,(1+L^2/\mu)^2\,(\bar\chi\sqrt{C_{+}}+\sqrt{C_{\star}})^2\;\bar\chi
}{
\varepsilon^{2}
}
\right),
\]
where $\widetilde{O}(\cdot)$ hides polylogarithmic factors in $t_{\mathrm{mix}}, L, \mu, \bar\chi, C_+, C_\star, 1/\varepsilon, \mathbb{E}[\Psi_0]$.
<!-- end theorem-8 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
At every $t$ applying Lemma~\ref{lem:ap-w-sample-complexity} with $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}=\frac{\varepsilon}{8(\bar\chi\sqrt{C_{+}}+\sqrt{C_{\star}})}$ we get the parameter choices stated in the theorem, and Assumption~\ref{as:stat-error} holds with this $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}$. The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:non-tabular-linear-convergence} uses the statistical error of the critic only through this assumption, in~\eqref{eq:e2_bound_sa}, so it does not require the critic calls, which share one trajectory, to be independent. By the precondition $\varepsilon\ge 8(\bar\chi\sqrt{C_+}+\sqrt{C_\star})\varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}}$ we have $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}}\le \frac{\varepsilon}{8(\bar\chi\sqrt{C_+}+\sqrt{C_\star})}$, so
\[
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}}\;\le\;\frac{\varepsilon}{4(\bar\chi\sqrt{C_+}+\sqrt{C_\star})}.
\]
The corresponding error floor in Theorem~\ref{thm:non-tabular-linear-convergence} then becomes
\[
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{floor}}
=
2(\bar\chi\sqrt{C_+}+\sqrt{C_\star})\big(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{app}}\big)
\;\le\;
\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.
\]
Applying Theorem~\ref{thm:non-tabular-linear-convergence} with $T \ge \left\lceil \bar\chi\log\!\Bigl(\frac{2\,\mathbb{E}[\Psi_0]}{\varepsilon}\Bigr)\right\rceil$ yields $\mathbb{E}[\Delta_T]\le \varepsilon$.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- proposition-4: body_md -->
\label{prop:ap-nontabular_lower_bound}
For every $d \geq 1$, $p \in (0, 1/4)$, and $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}} \in (0, 1/(256\,p)]$, there exists a family of one-action average-reward MDPs on $2d$ states with costs in $[0, 1]$ satisfying Assumption~\ref{as:ergodic}, whose $1/4$-mixing time satisfies $t_{\mathrm{mix}} \in [1/(16p),\, 3/p]$, and admitting an exactly realizable linear differential $Q$-function $Q^\theta(s,a) = w_\theta^{\star\top}\phi(s,a)$ with $\|\phi(s,a)\|_2 \leq 1$ and stationary covariance $\Sigma = \mathbb{E}_{\nu^\theta}[\phi\phi^\top] = (1/d)\,I_d$, such that any (possibly adaptive) generative-model algorithm that outputs $\widehat w \in \mathbb{R}^d$ with
\[
\Pr_\theta\!\left(\|\widehat w - w_\theta^\star\|_\Sigma^2 \leq \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2\right) \;\geq\; 2/3 \qquad \text{for every } \theta \in \{0, 1\}^d
\]
must use at least
\[
N \;\geq\; \frac{1}{2^{21}\cdot 27}\,\frac{d\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2} \;=\; \Omega\!\left(\frac{d\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2}\right)
\]
generative-model queries.
<!-- end proposition-4 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
The proof has six steps: construct the direct-sum reset family, verify its mixing properties, solve the centered Poisson equation, compute the per-coordinate gap and per-query KL, then assemble Assouad's inequality.

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Construction.} Fix $d \geq 1$ and $p \in (0, 1/4)$. Let $\mathcal S = [d] \times \{-1, +1\}$ with one action $\mathcal A = \{a\}$, set $\alpha := p$, and parameterize the family by $\theta \in \{0, 1\}^d$ via local transition rates $q_j(\theta) := p + \theta_j\, \Delta$, where $\Delta \in (0, p/2]$ will be fixed in Step~6. The transition kernel is
\[
P_\theta\bigl((j, \sigma),\,\cdot\bigr) \;:=\; (1 - \alpha)\,R_\theta\bigl((j, \sigma),\,\cdot\bigr) \;+\; \alpha\, u(\cdot),
\]
where $u$ is the uniform distribution on $\mathcal S$ (so $u(j, \sigma) = 1/(2d)$) and $R_\theta$ is the local sign-flip kernel
\[
R_\theta\bigl((j, \sigma),\,(j, \sigma)\bigr) \;:=\; 1 - q_j, \qquad R_\theta\bigl((j, \sigma),\,(j, -\sigma)\bigr) \;:=\; q_j,
\]
with $R_\theta((j, \sigma), \cdot) = 0$ on all other states. Cost $c(j, \sigma) := (1 + \sigma)/2 \in \{0, 1\}$ and features $\phi(j, \sigma) := \sigma\, e_j \in \mathbb{R}^d$.

 
  \@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@} 
                {1.5ex \@plus 0.5ex \@minus 0.2ex} 
                {-1em} 
                { 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize

  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize\normalsize\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt

  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
