\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Step 1: Stationary distribution.} The uniform distribution $u$ is invariant under $P_\theta$. Indeed, $u U = u$ trivially (where $U(s, \cdot) := u$ is the reset kernel), and for the local part,
\[
(u R_\theta)(k, \tau) \;=\; u(k, \tau)(1 - q_k) + u(k, -\tau)\, q_k \;=\; \tfrac{1}{2d}(1 - q_k) + \tfrac{1}{2d}\, q_k \;=\; \tfrac{1}{2d} \;=\; u(k, \tau).
\]
Hence $u P_\theta = (1 - \alpha)\, u + \alpha\, u = u$. Since $P_\theta(s, s') \geq \alpha\, u(s') = p/(2d) > 0$ for all $s, s'$, the chain is irreducible and aperiodic, so $d^\theta = u$ is the unique stationary distribution.

 
  \@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@} 
                {1.5ex \@plus 0.5ex \@minus 0.2ex} 
                {-1em} 
                { 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize

  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize\normalsize\@xpt\@xipt

  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip

\@xpt\@xipt

  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Step 2: Mixing time.} Decomposing $P_\theta = (1 - \alpha)\, R_\theta + \alpha\, U$ and using $u\, R_\theta = u$,
\[
P_\theta^k \;=\; (1 - \alpha)^k\, R_\theta^k \;+\; \bigl(1 - (1 - \alpha)^k\bigr)\, U,
\]
so $\mu P_\theta^k - u = (1-\alpha)^k(\mu R_\theta^k - u)$ and $\sup_\mu \|\mu P_\theta^k - u\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \leq (1-\alpha)^k$. Setting this $\leq 1/4$ and using $\log(1/(1-\alpha)) \geq \alpha$ gives the upper bound
\begin{equation} \label{eq:nlb-tmix-upper}
t_{\mathrm{mix}} \;\leq\; \lceil \log 4/\alpha \rceil \;\leq\; (\log 4 + 1)/\alpha \;<\; 3/\alpha \;=\; 3/p.
\end{equation}

For the lower bound, define the coordinate-local function $f_j(k, \tau) := \mathbf{1}\{k = j\}\, \tau$, which has $\|f_j\|_\infty \leq 1$ and $\mathbb{E}_u[f_j] = 0$. Direct computation shows $f_j$ is an eigenfunction of $P_\theta$ with eigenvalue $\lambda_j := (1 - \alpha)(1 - 2 q_j)$. Starting from $(j, +1)$,
\[
\bigl\|\delta_{(j, +1)}\, P_\theta^k - u\bigr\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \;\geq\; \tfrac{1}{2}\, \mathbb{E}_{(j, +1)}[f_j(X_k)] \;=\; \tfrac{1}{2}\,\lambda_j^k.
\]
With $\alpha = p$ and $q_j \leq p + \Delta \leq 3p/2$, $\lambda_j \geq (1-p)(1-3p) \geq 1 - 4p$ for $p \leq 1/16$. For $k \leq 1/(16p)$, $(1-4p)^k \geq \exp(-4pk/(1-4p)) \geq \exp(-1/3) > 1/2$. Hence for $p \leq 1/16$ and $k \leq 1/(16p)$,
\[
\bigl\|\delta_{(j, +1)}\, P_\theta^k - u\bigr\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \;>\; 1/4.
\]
For $p \in [1/16, 1/4)$, the trivial bound $t_{\mathrm{mix}} \geq 1$ implies $t_{\mathrm{mix}} \geq 1/(16p)$. Combining:
\[
1/(16p) \;\leq\; t_{\mathrm{mix}} \;\leq\; 3/p.
\]

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Step 3: Poisson equation and linear realizability.} Since $d^\theta = u$, the average cost is $\rho(\theta) = \mathbb{E}_u[c] = 1/2$. The centered Poisson equation $\rho + h^\theta = c + P_\theta h^\theta$ with $\sum_s u(s) h^\theta(s) = 0$ admits the ansatz $h^\theta(j, \sigma) = \beta_j(\theta)\, \sigma$ (the centering is automatic since $\sum_\sigma \sigma = 0$). Computing $(P_\theta h^\theta)(j, \sigma) = (1 - \alpha)(1 - 2 q_j)\, \beta_j\, \sigma$ (the reset term vanishes since $\mathbb{E}_u[h^\theta] = 0$) and substituting,
\[
\tfrac{1}{2} + \beta_j\, \sigma \;=\; \tfrac{1 + \sigma}{2} \;+\; (1 - \alpha)(1 - 2 q_j)\, \beta_j\, \sigma \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \beta_j(\theta) \;=\; \frac{1}{2\bigl(\alpha + 2 q_j(1 - \alpha)\bigr)}.
\]
With $\alpha = p$ and $q_j \in [p, 3p/2]$, the denominator lies in $[5p, 8p]$, so $\beta_j(\theta) \in [1/(8p),\, 1/(5p)] = \Theta(1/p)$.

Since there is one action, $Q^\theta(j, \sigma, a) = h^\theta(j, \sigma) = \beta_j(\theta)\, \sigma = w_\theta^{\star\top}\phi(j, \sigma)$ with $w_\theta^\star := (\beta_1(\theta), \dots, \beta_d(\theta))^\top$. The approximation error is exactly zero. The covariance matrix is
\[
\Sigma \;=\; \mathbb{E}_{\nu^\theta}[\phi\phi^\top] \;=\; \frac{1}{2d}\sum_{j=1}^d \sum_{\sigma}(\sigma e_j)(\sigma e_j)^\top \;=\; \frac{1}{d}\, I_d,
\]
giving $\|\phi\|_2 \leq 1$ ($L = 1$) and $\mu = 1/d$.

  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Step 4: Per-coordinate gap.} Write $\beta(q) := 1/(2p + 4q(1-p))$. Setting $D_0 := 2p + 4p(1-p) = p(6 - 4p) \in [5p, 6p]$ and $D_1 := 2p + 4(p+\Delta)(1-p) \in [5p, 8p]$ for $\Delta \leq p/2$,
\[
|\beta(p + \Delta) - \beta(p)| \;=\; \frac{4(1-p)\,\Delta}{D_0\, D_1} \;\geq\; \frac{3\,\Delta}{(6p)(8p)} \;=\; \frac{1}{16}\,\frac{\Delta}{p^2},
\]
where we used $4(1-p) \geq 3$ for $p \leq 1/4$. Define $\delta_w := |\beta(p + \Delta) - \beta(p)| \geq \Delta/(16 p^2)$.

 
  \@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@}

  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@

  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Step 5: Per-query KL.} Fix $\theta, \theta'$ differing only in coordinate $j$, with $q_j = p$ and $q_j' = p + \Delta$. A query from any state $(j', \sigma')$ with $j' \neq j$ has identical next-state law under both hypotheses (only $q_j$ changes), so the per-query KL is zero.

For a query from $(j, \sigma)$, the only changing atoms are $(j, \sigma)$ and $(j, -\sigma)$:
\begin{align*}
P_q\bigl((j, \sigma),\,(j, \sigma)\bigr) &\;=\; A(q) := s(1 - q) + p/(2d), \\
P_q\bigl((j, \sigma),\,(j, -\sigma)\bigr) &\;=\; B(q) := s\, q + p/(2d),
\end{align*}
where $s := 1 - p$. By the standard chi-square upper bound $\mathrm{KL}(P \| Q) \leq \sum_x (P(x) - Q(x))^2/Q(x)$,
\[
\mathrm{KL}(P_q \| P_{q'}) \;\leq\; s^2\, \Delta^2\!\left(\frac{1}{A(q')} + \frac{1}{B(q')}\right).
\]
For $p \leq 1/4$ and $\Delta \leq p/2$, $B(q') \geq sp \geq 3p/4$ (so $1/B(q') \leq 4/(3p)$) and $A(q') \geq s(1 - p - \Delta) \geq (3/4)(5/8) = 15/32$, hence $1/A(q') \leq 32/15 \leq 8/(15p)$ (using $p \leq 1/4$, so $1/p \geq 4$). Combining,
\[
\frac{1}{A(q')} + \frac{1}{B(q')} \;\leq\; \left(\frac{8}{15} + \frac{4}{3}\right)\frac{1}{p} \;=\; \frac{28}{15\,p} \;<\; \frac{2}{p}.
\]
Since $s^2 \leq 1$,
\begin{equation} \label{eq:nlb-kl-bound}
\mathrm{KL}\bigl(P_\theta(\cdot \mid j, \sigma) \,\big\|\, P_{\theta'}(\cdot \mid j, \sigma)\bigr) \;\leq\; 2\,\frac{\Delta^2}{p}.
\end{equation}

  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Step 6: Assouad assembly.} Suppose, for contradiction, an algorithm using at most $N$ generative-model queries satisfies the success condition. Let the transcript be $\mathcal T := (U, S_1, Y_1, \dots, S_N, Y_N, \widehat w)$, where $U$ is the algorithm's internal randomness, $S_n$ is the queried state at round $n$, and $Y_n$ is the observed next state. The query rule $S_n$ is measurable with respect to $(U, S_1, Y_1, \dots, S_{n-1}, Y_{n-1})$ and has the same conditional law under every $\theta$; only the next-state law $P_\theta(\cdot \mid S_n)$ depends on $\theta$. Hence the KL chain rule gives
\[
\mathrm{KL}\bigl(\mathbb{P}_\theta^{\mathcal T} \,\big\|\, \mathbb{P}_{\theta'}^{\mathcal T}\bigr) \;=\; \sum_{n=1}^N \mathbb{E}_\theta\!\left[\mathrm{KL}\bigl(P_\theta(\cdot \mid S_n) \,\big\|\, P_{\theta'}(\cdot \mid S_n)\bigr)\right].
\]
Letting $N_j := \#\{n : S_n \in \{j\} \times \{-1, +1\}\}$ be the number of queries to coordinate $j$ (so $\sum_j N_j \leq N$) and using~\eqref{eq:nlb-kl-bound} with zero cross-coordinate KL,
\[
\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_\theta \,\|\, \mathbb{P}_{\theta^{(j)}}) \;\leq\; 2\,\frac{\Delta^2}{p}\,\mathbb{E}_\theta[N_j],
\]
where $\theta^{(j)}$ is $\theta$ with bit $j$ flipped. Since $\beta(q) = 1/(2p + 4q(1-p))$ is \emph{decreasing} in $q$, we have $w_{\theta, j}^\star = \beta(p) > \beta(p + \Delta) = w_{\theta^{(j)}, j}^\star$ when $\theta_j = 0$. Accordingly, define the bit decoder
\[
\widehat\theta_j \;:=\; \mathbf{1}\!\left\{\widehat w_j < (\beta(p) + \beta(p + \Delta))/2\right\}.
\]
If $\widehat\theta_j \neq \theta_j$, then $|\widehat w_j - w_{\theta, j}^\star| \geq \delta_w/2$, so each Hamming error contributes $\delta_w^2/(4d)$ to $\|\widehat w - w_\theta^\star\|_\Sigma^2 = (1/d)\|\widehat w - w_\theta^\star\|_2^2$:
\begin{equation} \label{eq:nlb-hamming-loss}
d_H(\widehat\theta, \theta) \;\leq\; \frac{4 d}{\delta_w^2}\, \|\widehat w - w_\theta^\star\|_\Sigma^2.
\end{equation}

Choose $\Delta := 128\, p^2 \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}$. Then $\delta_w \geq \Delta/(16 p^2) = 8 \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}$, so $\delta_w^2 \geq 64\, \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2$. The constraint $\Delta \leq p/2$ becomes $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}} \leq 1/(256\, p)$, which is the stated regime. Under the success hypothesis $\Pr_\theta(\|\widehat w - w_\theta^\star\|_\Sigma^2 \leq \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2) \geq 2/3$ and using \eqref{eq:nlb-hamming-loss}:
\[
\mathbb{E}_\theta[d_H(\widehat\theta, \theta)] \;\leq\; \tfrac{2}{3} \cdot \tfrac{4 d\, \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2}{64\, \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2} + \tfrac{1}{3} \cdot d \;=\; \tfrac{2}{3} \cdot \tfrac{d}{16} + \tfrac{d}{3} \;=\; \tfrac{3 d}{8}.
\]
Averaging over $\theta \in \{0, 1\}^d$, the average Hamming risk satisfies $R_H(\widehat\theta) \leq 3 d/8$.

We use the standard Hamming-risk form of Assouad's inequality \citep{yu_assouad_1997}: pairing each $\theta$ with $\theta^{(j)}$ for each $j$ and applying Pinsker plus Jensen,
\[
R_H(\widehat\theta) \;\geq\; \frac{d}{2}\!\left[1 - \sqrt{\frac{1}{2 d\, 2^{d-1}}\sum_{j=1}^d \sum_{\theta : \theta_j = 0}\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_\theta \,\|\, \mathbb{P}_{\theta^{(j)}})}\right].
\]
By the per-query KL bound and $\sum_j N_j \leq N$, $\sum_{j} \sum_{\theta : \theta_j = 0} \mathbb{E}_\theta[N_j] \leq 2^d\, N$, so the expression under the square root is bounded by $2\, N\, \Delta^2/(d\, p)$, giving
\[
R_H(\widehat\theta) \;\geq\; \frac{d}{2}\!\left[1 - \sqrt{2\,\frac{N\, \Delta^2}{d\, p}}\right].
\]
If $N \leq d\, p/(128\, \Delta^2)$, the radicand is $\leq 1/64$, hence $R_H(\widehat\theta) \geq (d/2)(1 - 1/8) = 7 d/16$. But we showed $R_H(\widehat\theta) \leq 3 d/8 < 7 d/16$, a contradiction. Therefore
\[
N \;>\; \frac{d\, p}{128\, \Delta^2} \;=\; \frac{d\, p}{128 \cdot 128^2\, p^4\, \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2} \;=\; \frac{1}{2^{21}}\,\frac{d}{p^3\, \varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2}.
\]
Using $1/p^3 \geq t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3/27$ from \eqref{eq:nlb-tmix-upper}:
\[
N \;\geq\; \frac{1}{2^{21}\cdot 27}\,\frac{d\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}^3}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{stat}}^2}. \qedhere
\]
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem:per-policy-mixing}
Assume $\mathcal S$ is finite and that $P_\pi$ is irreducible and aperiodic for a fixed policy $\pi$. Then $t_{\mathrm{mix}}(\pi)$ defined by~\eqref{eq:per-policy-tmix} is finite.
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
Fix $\pi$. Since $P_\pi$ is finite-state, irreducible, and aperiodic, it is \emph{primitive}; hence there exists an integer $m_\pi \geq 1$ such that $P_\pi^{m_\pi}(s' \mid s) > 0$ for all $s, s' \in \mathcal S$ (see \citet[Proposition~1.7]{levin_markov_2017}). Let
\[
\varepsilon_\pi \;:=\; \min_{s, s' \in \mathcal S} P_\pi^{m_\pi}(s' \mid s) \;>\; 0, \qquad \alpha_\pi \;:=\; |\mathcal S|\, \varepsilon_\pi \in (0, 1],
\]
and let $\nu$ be the uniform distribution on $\mathcal S$. Then $P_\pi^{m_\pi}(\cdot \mid s) \geq \alpha_\pi\, \nu$ for every $s$. Defining the residual kernel $R_\pi(\cdot \mid s) := (P_\pi^{m_\pi}(\cdot \mid s) - \alpha_\pi\, \nu)/(1 - \alpha_\pi)$, we obtain $P_\pi^{m_\pi} = \alpha_\pi\, \mathbf{1}\, \nu + (1 - \alpha_\pi)\, R_\pi$, and by the same non-expansive argument as above,
\[
\sup_{\mu_0}\, \bigl\|\mu_0\, P_\pi^{q\, m_\pi} - d^\pi\bigr\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \;\leq\; (1 - \alpha_\pi)^q \qquad \forall\, q \geq 0.
\]
Choosing $q$ large enough that $(1 - \alpha_\pi)^q \leq 1/4$, namely $q \geq \lceil \log 4 / \log(1/(1-\alpha_\pi)) \rceil$, yields $t_{\mathrm{mix}}(\pi) \leq q\, m_\pi < \infty$.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
For $\pi \in \Pi$ and $k \geq 1$, write $\mathrm{TV}_\pi(k) := \sup_{\mu_0 \in \mathcal{P}(\mathcal S)} \|\mu_0\, P_\pi^k - d^\pi\|_{\mathrm{TV}}$. We use three facts.
\emph{(i) Monotonicity.} Since $d^\pi$ is stationary and $P_\pi$ is non-expansive in total variation, $\mathrm{TV}_\pi(k+1) \leq \mathrm{TV}_\pi(k)$.
\emph{(ii) Continuity.} For fixed $k$, the map $\mu_0 \mapsto \|\mu_0\, P_\pi^k - d^\pi\|_{\mathrm{TV}}$ is convex, so its supremum over the simplex is attained at a vertex, $\mathrm{TV}_\pi(k) = \max_{s \in \mathcal S} \|P_\pi^k(\cdot \mid s) - d^\pi\|_{\mathrm{TV}}$. Both $\pi \mapsto P_\pi^k$ and $\pi \mapsto d^\pi$ are continuous on $\Pi$ (the latter is shown in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:ap-uniform-d-bound}), so $\pi \mapsto \mathrm{TV}_\pi(k)$ is continuous as a finite maximum of continuous functions.
\emph{(iii) Pointwise decay.} For each $\pi$, the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:per-policy-mixing} gives $\mathrm{TV}_\pi(q\, m_\pi) \leq (1 - \alpha_\pi)^q \to 0$, so there is $k_\pi \geq 1$ with $\mathrm{TV}_\pi(k_\pi) < 1/4$.

By (ii), $U_\pi := \{\pi' \in \Pi : \mathrm{TV}_{\pi'}(k_\pi) < 1/4\}$ is an open neighborhood of $\pi$. Since $\Pi = \mathcal{P}(\mathcal A)^{\mathcal S}$ is compact, finitely many $U_{\pi_1}, \dots, U_{\pi_n}$ cover it. Let $t_{\mathrm{mix}} := \max_i k_{\pi_i}$. Any $\pi' \in \Pi$ lies in some $U_{\pi_i}$, and (i) gives $\mathrm{TV}_{\pi'}(t_{\mathrm{mix}}) \leq \mathrm{TV}_{\pi'}(k_{\pi_i}) < 1/4$.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- head-1: body_md -->
With $\mathrm{TV}_\pi(k)$ as in the proof above, let $\overline{\mathrm{TV}}_\pi(k) := \sup_{\mu, \mu' \in \mathcal{P}(\mathcal S)} \|\mu\, P_\pi^k - \mu'\, P_\pi^k\|_{\mathrm{TV}}$. Then $\mathrm{TV}_\pi \leq \overline{\mathrm{TV}}_\pi \leq 2\,\mathrm{TV}_\pi$, and $\overline{\mathrm{TV}}_\pi$ is submultiplicative, $\overline{\mathrm{TV}}_\pi(k + l) \leq \overline{\mathrm{TV}}_\pi(k)\, \overline{\mathrm{TV}}_\pi(l)$ \citep[Lemmas~4.10--4.11]{levin_markov_2017}. The first part of Lemma~\ref{lem:uniform-mix} gives $\overline{\mathrm{TV}}_\pi(t_{\mathrm{mix}}) \leq 2\,\mathrm{TV}_\pi(t_{\mathrm{mix}}) \leq 1/2$, so $\mathrm{TV}_\pi(\ell\, t_{\mathrm{mix}}) \leq \overline{\mathrm{TV}}_\pi(t_{\mathrm{mix}})^\ell \leq 2^{-\ell}$ for every integer $\ell \geq 0$. Since $\mathrm{TV}_\pi$ is nonincreasing by (i), $\mathrm{TV}_\pi(k) \leq 2^{-\lfloor k/t_{\mathrm{mix}} \rfloor}$ for all $k \geq 0$, which is~\eqref{eq:tmix-decay}. The factor $1/2$ per block, rather than the $1/4$ at $k = t_{\mathrm{mix}}$, is the price of a single bound valid for every $k$.
<!-- end head-1 -->
<!-- head-2: body_md -->
We run KL-PMD,
\[
\pi^{t+1}(a\mid s)\;\propto\;\pi^{t}(a\mid s)\exp\!\bigl(-\eta_t\,\widehat Q^t(s,a)\bigr),
\]
where $\widehat Q^t$ is either the exact differential action-value
$Q^{\pi^t}$ (Section~\ref{ap:exp-exact}) or a noisy approximation
(Section~\ref{ap:exp-inexact}). At each iteration we compute $Q^{\pi^t}$
exactly by solving the Poisson equation for $h^{\pi^t}$ in
\eqref{eq:ap-poisson} with the anchor $h^{\pi^t}(0)=0$.
<!-- end head-2 -->
<!-- head-3: body_md -->
Across all four families the median trajectory
collapses by roughly $10\text{--}1000\times$ within the first
$\sim 20\text{--}40$ iterations and remains essentially flat thereafter. The
transient $\bar\chi$ regime that drives
Theorem~\ref{thm:avg-pmd-linear-inexact-limsup}'s non-asymptotic bound
is therefore a brief warm-up across problem types of widely varying
state-action sizes, transition structures, and optimal-policy
geometry; the asymptotic regime of Corollary~\ref{cor:exact-pmd}, where
$\chi$ governs convergence, is the operationally relevant one.
<!-- end head-3 -->
